EduPulse Live – Event-Driven Social Learning Hub\n\n![CI](https://github.com/edupulse/live/workflows/CI/badge.svg)\n![License](https://img.shields.io/github/license/edupulse/live)\n![Rust](https://img.shields.io/badge/Rust-1.74%2B-orange)\n\nEduPulse Live is an expert-level, event-driven social network for educators and learners that blends course discussions, real-time collaboration, and academic resource sharing.  \nEvery domain concept—lesson publication, quiz submission, peer feedback, badge awards, or payment for premium micro-courses—**emits domain events** that flow through a message broker, keeping the core HTTP API lean and highly responsive.\n\n> TL;DR  \n> `cargo run --package web_social` will spin up the API on <https://localhost:8080>, publish domain events to **RabbitMQ**, persist state in **PostgreSQL**, index resources in **MeiliSearch**, and serve a blazing-fast interactive SPA via **Trunk + Wasm**.\n\n---\n\n## ✨ Key Capabilities\n\n| Category            | Highlights                                                                              |\n|---------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n| Real-time Learning  | “Learning pulses”, live Q&A threads, WebSocket rooms, in-browser code runners           |\n| Event-Driven Core   | Every aggregate action produces a strongly-typed CloudEvent                             |\n| Search & Discovery  | Incremental MeiliSearch indexing via asynchronous workers                               |\n| Achievements        | Badge / XP engine powered by event-sourced projections                                  |\n| Compliance          | GDPR “right to be forgotten” implemented as compensating erase events                   |\n| Secure by Default   | TLS-terminated, OAuth2 (Google EDU, Microsoft), rotating Redis sessions, rate limiting  |\n\n---\n\n## 🏗️  Tech Stack\n\n* **Rust 1.74+**   – Actix Web, Tokio, SeaORM  \n* **RabbitMQ 3.12** – AMQP 0-9-1 event bus  \n* **PostgreSQL 15** – primary data store, CQRS projections  \n* **MeiliSearch 1.4** – full-text search  \n* **Redis 7**        – cache + session store  \n* **React 18 + WASM** – front-end (see `/frontend`)  \n\n---\n\n## 🚀 Quick Start\n\n```bash\ngit clone https://github.com/edupulse/live.git\ncd live\n\n# 1. Configure environment variables\ncp .env.example .env\nvi .env           # adjust DB urls, secrets, etc.\n\n# 2. Database & broker\ndocker compose up -d postgres rabbitmq redis meilisearch\n\n# 3. Apply migrations\ncargo install sea-orm-cli\nsea migrate up\n\n# 4. Run the whole stack (API + workers)\ncargo make dev         # dev profile with hot-reloading (`cargo-make` is a dev-dependency)\n\n# 5. Hit the health-check\ncurl -k https://localhost:8080/healthz\n```\n\n`make dev` orchestrates:\n\n* `web_social` (Actix HTTP API)\n* `social_worker` (background AMQP consumer)\n* `trunk serve` (front-end local dev)\n\n---\n\n## 🗂️ Repository Layout\n\n```\n.\n├── crates\n│   ├── domain          # Domain aggregates, value objects, events\n│   ├── web_social      # REST/WS API crate (Actix)\n│   ├── social_worker   # Async workers (search, mail, achievements…)\n│   └── shared          # Cross-cutting (error types, tracing, config)\n├── frontend            # React + WASM widgets\n└── deploy              # Helm charts, Terraform, GitHub Actions\n```\n\n---\n\n## 🧩 Architecture Primer\n\nWe follow a pragmatic **Service-Layer + Repository Pattern** with clean separation of concerns.\n\n```mermaid\ngraph LR\nA[HTTP / WS] -->|DTO| B(API Controller)\nB -->|call| C(Service Layer)\nC -->|persist| D{Repository}\nC -->|publish| E[Domain Event]\nE -->|AMQP| F[Worker]\nF -->|side-effect| G[MeiliSearch / Email / Analytics]\n```\n\n### 1. Domain Event Definition\n\n```rust\n// crates/domain/src/event/pulse.rs\nuse chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse uuid::Uuid;\n\n/// CloudEvent-compliant wrapper for all domain events\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct CloudEvent<T>\nwhere\n    T: Serialize,\n{\n    pub id: Uuid,\n    pub source: String,\n    pub specversion: String,\n    pub r#type: String,\n    pub datacontenttype: String,\n    pub time: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub data: T,\n}\n\n/// Specific payload for a LearningPulseCreated event\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct LearningPulseCreated {\n    pub pulse_id: Uuid,\n    pub author_id: Uuid,\n    pub title: String,\n    pub expires_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Service Layer Publishes Events\n\n```rust\n// crates/web_social/src/service/pulse_service.rs\nuse domain::event::{CloudEvent, LearningPulseCreated};\nuse shared::{\n    amqp::Publisher,\n    db::RepoPool,\n    errors::ServiceError,\n};\n\npub struct PulseService {\n    repo: RepoPool,\n    publisher: Publisher,\n}\n\nimpl PulseService {\n    pub async fn create_pulse(&self, cmd: CreatePulse) -> Result<PulseDto, ServiceError> {\n        let pulse = self.repo.pulse().insert(&cmd).await?;\n        let event = CloudEvent::from_payload(\n            \"com.edupulse.pulse.created\",\n            &LearningPulseCreated {\n                pulse_id: pulse.id,\n                author_id: pulse.author_id,\n                title: pulse.title.clone(),\n                expires_at: pulse.expires_at,\n            },\n        );\n        self.publisher.publish(event).await?;\n        Ok(PulseDto::from(pulse))\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 3. Worker Listens & Acts\n\n```rust\n// crates/social_worker/src/handlers/search_index.rs\nuse domain::event::LearningPulseCreated;\nuse shared::{amqp::Consumer, search::SearchIndexer};\n\npub async fn start_search_listener(consumer: Consumer, indexer: SearchIndexer) {\n    consumer\n        .listen::<LearningPulseCreated>(\"com.edupulse.pulse.created\", move |evt| {\n            let idx = indexer.clone();\n            async move {\n                idx.index_pulse(evt.data).await?;\n                Ok(())\n            }\n        })\n        .await\n        .expect(\"Search listener crashed\");\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## 🔒 Security Notes\n\n1. All external traffic is forced through **TLS 1.3** (`rustls`, HSTS, OCSP stapling).  \n2. Cookies: `__Secure-sid`, `SameSite=Lax`, 15 min idle, 8 hour absolute, rotated every login.  \n3. Input validation enforced at DTO boundaries (`validator` crate).  \n4. **Brute-force guard** via Redis-backed sliding window rate limiter.  \n5. =============================================================================================\n#[async_trait]\npub trait UserRepository: Send + Sync {\n    async fn insert(&self, user: &User) -> async fn insert(&self, user: &User) -> Result<()> {\n        sqlx::query(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO users (id, email, password_hash, created_at)\n            VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4)\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(user.id)\n        .bind(&user.email)\n        .bind(&user.password_hash)\n        .bind(user.created_at)\n        .execute(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    async fn find_by_email(&self, email: &str) -> Result<Option<User>> {\n        let maybe_row = sqlx::query(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT id, email, password_hash, =============================================================================================\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize)]\npub enum DomainEvent {\n    UserRegistered { user_id: Uuid, email: String },\n    UserLoggedIn { user_id: Uuid },\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventBus: Send + Sync {\n    async fn publish(&self, evt: &DomainEvent) -> anyhow::Result<()>;\n}\n\n/// A naive, logging-only event bus implementation. Replace with Kafka/NATS/RabbitMQ as needed.\npub struct TracingEventBus;\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventBus for TracingEventBus {\n    async fn publish(&self, evt: &DomainEvent) -> anyhow::Result<()> {\n        info!(?evt, \"domain event published\");\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/// =============================================================================================\n/// Session cache Some(std::time::Duration::from_secs(3));\n\n        let pool = cfg.create_pool(Some(deadpool_redis::Runtime::Tokio1))?;\n        Ok(Self { pool })\n    }\n\n    async fn put(&self, token: &str, user_id: Uuid, ttl: Duration) -> anyhow::Result<()> {\n        let mut conn: Connection = self.pool.get().await?;\n        cmd(\"SET\")\n            .arg(token)\n            .arg(user_id.to_string())\n            .arg(\"EX\")\n            .arg(ttl.num_seconds())\n            .query_async(&mut conn)\n            .await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    #[allow(dead_code)]\n    async fn get_user_id(&self, token: &str) -> anyhow::Result<Option<Uuid>> {\n        let mut conn: Connection = self.pool.get().await?;\n        let resp: Option<String> = conn.get(token).await?;\n        Ok(resp.and_then(|s| Uuid::parse_str(&s).ok()))\n    }\n\n    async fn delete(&self, token: &str) -> anyhow::Result<()> {\n        let mut conn: Connection = self.pool.get().await?;\n        conn.del(token).await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/// =============================================================================================\n/// Service layer\n/// SessionStore, jwt_secret: String) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            repo,\n            event_bus,\n            session_store,\n            jwt_secret,\n            session_ttl: Duration::hours(12),\n            argon2: Argon2::default(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Registers a new user, returning a newly minted [`Session`] on success.\n    pub async fn register_user(&self, new_user: NewUser) -> Result<Session> {\n        if self.repo.find_by_email(&new_user.email).await?.is_some() {\n            return Err(AuthError::InvalidCredentials); // simplified: email already taken\n        }\n\n        let salt = SaltString::generate(&mut rand::thread_rng());\n        let password_hash = self\n            .argon2\n            .hash_password(new_user.password.as_bytes(),       };\n\n        self.repo.insert(&user).await?;\n        self.event_bus\n            .publish(&DomainEvent::UserRegistered {\n                user_id: user.id,\n                email: .await?\n            .ok_or(AuthError::InvalidCredentials)?;\n\n        // Verify password\n        let parsed_hash = PasswordHash::new(&user.password_hash)?;\n        self.argon2\n            .verify_password(password.as_bytes(), &parsed_hash)\n            .map_err(|_| AuthError::InvalidCredentials)?;\n\n        let session = self.create_session(user.id).await?;\n\n        self.event_bus\n            .publish(&DomainEvent::UserLoggedIn { user_id: user.id })\n            .await?;\n\n        Ok(session)\n    }\n\n    /// Invalidates an existing session.\n    pub async fn    }\n\n    /// Helper: create a JWT token + Redis cache entry.\n    async fn create_session(&self, user_id: Uuid) -> Result<Session> {\n        let now = Utc::now();\n        let exp = (now + self.session_ttl).timestamp() as usize;\n\n        let claims = Claims {\n            sub: user_id.to_string(),\n            exp,\n            iat: now.timestamp() as usize,\n        };\n\n        let header = Header::default();\n        let token = encode(\n            &header,\n            &claims,\n            &EncodingKey::from_secret(self.jwt_secret.as_bytes()),\n        )?;\n\n        // Cache in Redis for server-side revocation\n        self.session_store\n            .put(&token, user_id, self.session_ttl)\n            .await\n            .map_err(|e| AuthError::EventBus(e.into()))?;\n\n        Ok(Session {\n            token,\n            expires_at: now + self.session_ttl,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Validate a token and return the user id embedded in it.\n    pub async fn validate_token(&self, token: &str) -> Result<Uuid> {\n        // 1. Verify signature / expiration\n        let TokenData { claims, .. } = decode::<Claims>(\n            token,\n            &DecodingKey::from_secret(self.jwt_secret.as_bytes()),\n            &Validation::default(),\n        )?;\n\n        // 2. Check server-side revocation list (Redis)\n        if self\n            .session_store\n            .get_user_id(token)\n            .await\n            .map_err(|e| AuthError::EventBus(e.into()))?\n            .is_none()\n        {\n            return Err(AuthError::InvalidCredentials);\n        }\n\n        let uuid = Uuid::parse_str(&claims.sub).map_err(|_| AuthError::InvalidCredentials)?;\n        Ok(uuid)\n    }\n}\n\n/// =============================================================================================\n/// REST handler glue (simplified for brevity). In a real project, this would live in actix-web or\n/// axum route modules. Here we show just enough to demonstrate SVC integration.\n/// =============================================================================================\n#[cfg(feature = \"rest_api\")]\npub mod rest {\n    use super::*;\n    use axum::{\n        extract::{Extension, Json},\n        http::StatusCode,\n        response::IntoResponse,\n        routing::post,\n        Router,\n    };\n    use std::sync::Arc;\n\n    #[derive(Clone)]\n    pub struct AppState<A> {\n        pub auth_service: Arc<A>,\n    }\n\n    pub fn router<A: 'static + Send + Sync>(\n        state: AppState<A>,\n    ) -> Router\n    where\n        A: AuthServiceTrait,\n    {\n        Router::new()\n            .route(\"/api/v1/auth/register\", post(register::<A>))\n            .route(\"/api/v1/auth/login\", post(login::<A>))\n            .layer(Extension(state))\n    }\n\n    #[async_trait]\n    pub trait AuthServiceTrait: Send + Sync {\n        async fn register(&self, new_user: NewUser) -> Result<Session>;\n        async fn login(&self, email: &str, password: &str) -> Result<Session>;\n    }\n\n    #[async_trait]\n    impl<R, B> AuthServiceTrait for super::AuthService<R, B>\n    where\n        R: UserRepository + 'static,\n        B: EventBus + 'static,\n    {\n        async fn register(&self, new_user: NewUser) -> Result<Session> {\n            self.register_user(new_user).await\n        }\n\n        async fn login(&self, email: &str, password: &str) -> Result<Session> {\n            self.login_user(email, password).await\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// ----------------------------------------\n    /// Route handlers\n    /// ----------------------------------------\n    async fn register<A>(\n        Json(payload): Json<NewUser>,\n        Extension(state): Extension<AppState<A>>,\n    ) -> impl IntoResponse\n    where\n        A: AuthServiceTrait,\n    {\n        match state.auth_service.register(payload).await {\n            Ok(session) => (StatusCode::CREATED, Json(session)).into_response(),\n            Err(e) => {\n                error!(\"registration error: {e}\");\n                (StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST, Json(e.to_string())).into_response()\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[derive(Deserialize)]\n    struct LoginPayload {\n        email: String,\n        password: String,\n    }\n\n    async fn login<A>(\n        Json(payload): Json<LoginPayload>,\n        Extension(state): Extension<AppState<A>>,\n    ) -> impl IntoResponse\n    where\n        A: AuthServiceTrait,\n    {\n        match         Err(e) => {\n                error!(\"login error: {e}\");\n                (StatusCode::UNAUTHORIZED, Json(e.to_string())).into_response()\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// AuthService<PostgresUserRepository, TracingEventBus>,\n> {\n    let database_url =\n        env::var(\"DATABASE_URL\").expect(\"DATABASE_URL must be set (postgres connection string)\");\n    let random key)\");\n\n    let pool = PgPool::connect(&database_url).await?;\n\n    let repo = PostgresUserRepository::new(pool);\n    let bus = TracingEventBus;\n    let session_store = SessionStore::new(&redis_url)?;\n\n    Ok(AuthService::new(repo, bus, session_store, jwt_secret))\n}\n```","numLines":512,"startLine":1,"totalLines":512}}}

Each variant is versioned so we can evolve payloads\n/// without breaking existing subscribers.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(tag = \"type\", content = \"payload\")]\npub enum DomainEvent {\n    /// A teacher has published a new lesson.\n    LessonPublishedV1 {\n        lesson_id: Uuid,\n        title: String,\n        author_id: Uuid,\n    },\n\n    /// A student submitted answers to a quiz.\n    QuizSubmittedV1 {\n        quiz_id: Uuid,\n        attempt_id: Uuid,\n        student_id: Uuid,\n    },\n\n    /// A peer gave feedback to someone else's submission.\n    PeerFeedbackGivenV1 {\n        feedback_id: Uuid,\n        submission_id: Uuid,\n        reviewer_id: Uuid,\n    },\n\n    /// A student was granted a badge.\n    BadgeAwardedV1 {\n        badge_id: Uuid,\n        awardee_id: Uuid,\n        reason: String,\n    },\n\n    /// Money changed hands for a premium micro-course.\n    PaymentCapturedV1 {\n        payment_id: Uuid,\n        amount_cents: i64,\n        currency: String,\n        payer_id: Uuid,\n    },\n}\n\n/// Envelope that wraps the event payload with universal metadata.\n///\n/// Storing metadata *outside* the payload makes it trivial to evolve individual\n/// events without duplicating the same bookkeeping fields everywhere.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct EventEnvelope {\n    pub meta: EventMetadata,\n    pub event: DomainEvent,\n}\n\nimpl EventEnvelope {\n    pub fn new<E: Into<DomainEvent>>(event: E) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            meta: EventMetadata::default(),\n            event: event.into(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Serialise the envelope into a compact JSON payload.\n///\n/// We purposefully avoid pretty printing for network efficiency; producers that\n/// want human-readable output can call `pretty_json()` instead.\nimpl EventEnvelope {\n    pub fn to_json_bytes(&self) -> Result<Vec<u8>, EventError> {\n        Ok(serde_json::to_vec(self)?)\n    }\n\n    #[allow(dead_code)]\n    pub fn pretty_json(&self) -> Result<String, EventError> {\n        Ok(serde_json::to_string_pretty(self)?)\n    }\n\n    /// Derive the *logical* routing key used in AMQP brokers.  Topic exchange\n    /// policies can fan-out events to multiple queues, e.g. `lesson.published.v1`.\n    pub fn routing_key(&self) -> String {\n        match &self.event {\n            DomainEvent::LessonPublishedV1 { .. } => \"lesson.published.v1\",\n            DomainEvent::QuizSubmittedV1 { .. } => \"quiz.submitted.v1\",\n            DomainEvent::PeerFeedbackGivenV1 { .. } => \"feedback.given.v1\",\n            DomainEvent::BadgeAwardedV1 { .. } => \"badge.awarded.v1\",\n            DomainEvent::PaymentCapturedV1 { .. } => \"payment.captured.v1\",\n        }\n        .into()\n    }\n}\n\n/// Error type for publishing and serialisation failures.\n#[derive(Error, Debug)]\npub enum EventError {\n    #[error(\"serialization error: {0}\")]\n    Serialization(#[from] serde_json::Error),\n\n    #[cfg(feature = \"broker\")]\n    #[error(\"amqp error: {0}\")]\n    Amqp(#[from] lapin::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"broker confirmation timed out\")]\n    ConfirmationTimeout,\n\n    #[error(\"other: {0}\")]\n    Other(String),\n}\n\n/// Trait every publisher (mock or real) must implement.\n///\n/// The abstraction enables dependency injection in application services and\n/// makes *unit* testing simpler:  we can just inject the `LoggingPublisher`.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventPublisher: Send + Sync {\n    async fn publish(&self, envelope: EventEnvelope) -> Result<(), EventError>;\n}\n\n/// A publisher that merely writes events to the `log` sink.\n///\n/// Enabled by default, also used when the `broker` feature is disabled. The\n/// design means the rest of the application can always depend on\n/// `dyn EventPublisher`, regardless of compilation mode.\npub struct LoggingPublisher;\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventPublisher for LoggingPublisher {\n    async fn publish(&self, envelope: EventEnvelope) -> Result<(), EventError> {\n        // Format into single-line JSON to keep log ingestion systems happy.\n        let json = envelope.to_json_bytes()?;\n        // Safe because JSON is UTF-8.\n        log::info!(\n            target: \"eduplse.events\",\n            \"event={} meta_id={} payload={}\",\n            envelope.routing_key(),\n            envelope.meta.id,\n            String::from_utf8_lossy(&json)\n        );\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/// Real AMQP publisher that talks to RabbitMQ.  \n///\n/// Compiled **only** when the `broker` feature is present.  Uses publisher\n/// confirms so we know the broker durably persisted the message.\n#[cfg(feature = \"broker\")]\npub struct RabbitMqPublisher {\n    channel: Channel,\n    exchange: String,\n}\n\n#[cfg(feature = \"broker\")]\nimpl RabbitMqPublisher {\n    /// Connect to the AMQP broker and prepare the channel.\n    ///\n    /// A production-grade implementation would configure TLS, heartbeat, and\n    /// automatic reconnection. Those details are omitted for brevity.\n    pub async fn connect(\n        amqp_uri: &str,\n        exchange: &str,\n    ) -> Result<Self, EventError> {\n        let connection = Connection::connect(\n            amqp_uri,\n            ConnectionProperties::default().with_default_executor(8),\n        )\n        .await\n        .map_err(EventError::from)?;\n\n        let channel = connection.create_channel().await?;\n        channel\n            .confirm_select(lapin::options::ConfirmSelectOptions::default())\n            .await?;\n\n        Ok(Self {\n            channel,\n            exchange: exchange.to_owned(),\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(feature = \"broker\")]\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventPublisher for RabbitMqPublisher {\n    async fn publish(&self, envelope: EventEnvelope) -> Result<(), EventError> {\n        let payload = envelope.to_json_bytes()?;\n        let routing_key = envelope.routing_key();\n\n        let confirm: Confirmation = self\n            .channel\n            .basic_publish(\n                &self.exchange,\n                &routing_key,\n                BasicPublishOptions {\n                    mandatory: false,\n                    immediate: false,\n                },\n                &payload,\n                BasicProperties::default().with_content_type(\"application/json\"),\n            )\n            .await?\n            .await?; // Wait for broker confirm (publisher confirms must be enabled)\n\n        // lapin returns Nack(…) in error path; we check for `Ack`.\n        if !confirm.is_ack() {\n            return Err(EventError::Other(format!(\n                \"broker did not acknowledge event id {}\",\n                envelope.meta.id\n            )));\n        }\n\n        // Additionally enforce a timeout because `await?` can hang if broker is\n        // misconfigured (e.g., confirm_select was forgotten).\n        timeout(PUBLISH_CONFIRM_TIMEOUT, async {\n            while !confirm.is_ack() {}\n        })\n        .await\n        .map_err(|_| EventError::ConfirmationTimeout)?;\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/// When compiled **without** the `broker` feature, alias [`LoggingPublisher`]\n/// to `DefaultPublisher` to satisfy DI containers effortlessly.\n///\n/// ```\n/// use eduplse::events::DefaultPublisher;\n/// let publisher = DefaultPublisher;\n/// ```\n#[cfg(not(feature = \"broker\"))]\npub type DefaultPublisher = LoggingPublisher;\n\n#[cfg(feature = \"broker\")]\npub type DefaultPublisher = RabbitMqPublisher;\n\n//\n// Centralised domain-event infrastructure for EduPulse Live.\n//!\n//! This module exposes:\n//!   * DomainEvent & DomainEventKind – strongly-typed, versioned event\n//!     definitions shared across bounded contexts.\n//!   * EventPublisher – service-layer abstraction allowing the core API to\n//!     remain ignorant of transport details (NATS, Kafka, etc.).\n//!   * NatsEventPublisher – production implementation that publishes JSON\n//!     envelopes to a NATS JetStream subject hierarchy.\n//!   * EventStore – ACID append-only persistence used for audit trail / GDPR\n//!     erase workflow.\n//!   * PostgresEventStore – repository implementation via `sqlx`.\n//!   * LearningPulseService – sample use-case that persists a new learning\n//!     pulse and emits the corresponding event within a single transaction.\n//!\n//! The code follows Repository + Service-Layer patterns and is intended to be\n//! wired up by the dependency-injection crate of your choice (e.g. `shuttle`,\n//! `axum::extract::State`, or `tower` layers).\n//!\n//! # Crate features required\n//! Make sure `Cargo.toml` enables the following dependencies:\n//!\n//! ```toml\n//! [dependencies]\n//! async-nats = { version = \"0.34\", default-features = false, features = [\"jetstream\"] }\n//! async-trait = \"0.1\"\n//! anyhow = \"1\"\n//! chrono = { version = \"0.4\", features = [\"serde\"] }\n//! serde = { version = \"1.0\", features = [\"derive\"] }\n//! serde_json = \"1.0\"\n//! sqlx = { version = \"0.7\", default-features = false, features = [\"runtime-tokio-rustls\", \"postgres\", \"chrono\", \"uuid\", \"json\"] }\n//! tokio = { version = \"1\", features = [\"macros\", \"rt-multi-thread\"] }\n//! tracing = \"0.1\"\n//! uuid = { version = \"1\", features = [\"serde\", \"v4\"] }\n//! ```\n//!\n//! ## Warning\n//! Production environments should tune JetStream options (e.g., stream\n//! retention), connection retries, and TLS settings. This sample is simplified\n//! for readability.\n\nuse std::sync::Arc;\n\nuse anyhow::{Context, Result};\nuse async_nats::jetstream::{self, stream::Stream};\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse sqlx::{postgres::PgQueryResult, PgPool, Postgres, Transaction};\nuse tracing::{debug, error, info, instrument};\nuse uuid::Uuid;\n\n// -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Domain Event Definitions\n// -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// Enum representing all event payloads produced by the **web_social** bounded\n/// context. Each variant must remain strictly additive (append-only) to avoid\n/// breaking consumers performing pattern matching.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(tag = \"type\", content = \"data\")]\n#[non_exhaustive]\npub enum DomainEventKind {\n    LessonPublished {\n        lesson_id: Uuid,\n        teacher_id: Uuid,\n    },\n    QuizSubmitted {\n        quiz_id: Uuid,\n        student_id: Uuid,\n        score_percentage: f32,\n    },\n    BadgeAwarded {\n        badge_id: Uuid,\n        student_id: Uuid,\n    },\n    PaymentProcessed {\n        payment_id: Uuid,\n        order_total_cents: i64,\n        payer_id: Uuid,\n    },\n    LearningPulseCreated {\n        pulse_id: Uuid,\n        teacher_id: Uuid,\n    },\n    LearningPulseResponded {\n        pulse_id: Uuid,\n        student_id: Uuid,\n        response_id: Uuid,\n    },\n}\n\n/// Envelope that wraps every event with rich metadata required for correlation\n/// and causation analysis.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct DomainEvent {\n    /// Unique ID for this event. Uses UUID v4 for randomness and horizontal\n    /// partitioning friendliness.\n    pub id: Uuid,\n    /// ID that allows grouping of events that belong to the same workflow.\n    pub correlation_id: Option<Uuid>,\n    /// ID of the event that directly triggered this one, if any.\n    pub causation_id: Option<Uuid>,\n    /// Actor responsible (user, system) – useful for audit trail.\n    pub actor_id: Option<Uuid>,\n    /// Time of creation in UTC with millisecond precision.\n    pub occurred_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    /// Semantic version of the payload schema.\n    pub schema_version: u16,\n    /// Actual business payload.\n    pub payload: DomainEventKind,\n}\n\nimpl DomainEvent {\n    /// Creates a new envelope, stamping with `Utc::now()`.\n    pub fn new(actor_id: Option<Uuid>, payload: DomainEventKind) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            correlation_id: None,\n            causation_id: None,\n            actor_id,\n            occurred_at: Utc::now(),\n            schema_version: 1,\n            payload,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Returns the recommended NATS subject where this event should be\n    /// published, following the `edupulse.{bounded_context}.{event_name}` style\n    /// to facilitate fine-grained subscriptions.\n    pub fn subject(&self) -> String {\n        let event_name = match &self.payload {\n            DomainEventKind::LessonPublished { .. } => \"lesson_published\",\n            DomainEventKind::QuizSubmitted { .. } => \"quiz_submitted\",\n            DomainEventKind::BadgeAwarded { .. } => \"badge_awarded\",\n            DomainEventKind::PaymentProcessed { .. } => \"payment_processed\",\n            DomainEventKind::LearningPulseCreated { .. } => \"learning_pulse_created\",\n            DomainEventKind::LearningPulseResponded { .. } => \"learning_pulse_responded\",\n        };\n        format!(\"edupulse.web_social.{}\", event_name)\n    }\n}\n\n// -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// EventPublisher Abstraction\n// -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// Transport-agnostic publisher for domain events. Implementations must be\n/// idempotent and **MUST NOT** panic; failures are returned as `Err`.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventPublisher: Send + Sync {\n    async fn publish(&self, event: &DomainEvent) -> Result<()>;\n}\n\n// -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// NATS JetStream Implementation\n// -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// Production-grade NATS JetStream publisher, supporting at-least-once delivery.\n/// Uses JSON serialisation for maximum interoperability with polyglot services.\npub struct NatsEventPublisher {\n    client: async_nats::Client,\n    jetstream: jetstream::Context,\n    stream: Stream,\n}\n\nimpl NatsEventPublisher {\n    /// Creates a new publisher, auto-provisioning the `edupulse` stream if it\n    /// does not exist. The stream is configured for message-size budgets\n    /// typical to domain events (< 64 KiB).\n    pub async fn new(nats_url: &str) -> Result<Self> {\n        let client = async_nats::connect(nats_url).await?;\n        let jetstream = jetstream::new(client.clone());\n\n        let stream = match jetstream.get_stream(\"EDUPULSE\").await {\n            Ok(stream) => stream,\n            Err(_) => {\n                info!(\"creating JetStream stream `EDUPULSE`\");\n                jetstream\n                    .create_stream(jetstream::stream::Config {\n                        name: \"EDUPULSE\".to_owned(),\n                        subjects: vec![\"edupulse.>\".to_owned()],\n                        max_age: chrono::Duration::days(7).to_std().unwrap(),\n                        storage: jetstream::stream::StorageType::File,\n                        ..Default::default()\n                    })\n                    .await?\n            }\n        };\n\n        Ok(Self {\n            client,\n            jetstream,\n            stream,\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventPublisher for NatsEventPublisher {\n    #[instrument(skip_all, fields(event_id = %event.id, subject = %event.subject()))]\n    async fn publish(&self, event: &DomainEvent) -> Result<()> {\n        let bytes = serde_json::to_vec(event)?;\n        self.client\n            .publish(event.subject(), bytes.into())\n            .await\n            .context(\"failed to publish event to NATS\")?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n// -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Event Store Repository\n// -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// Append-only store for domain events, enabling event sourcing and audit trail\n/// reconstruction. Each implementation should ensure **exactly-once** writes.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventStore: Send + Sync {\n    async fn append(&self, tx: &mut Transaction<'_, Postgres>, event: &DomainEvent)\n        -> Result<()>;\n    async fn list_by_aggregate(\n        &self,\n        aggregate_id: Uuid,\n        limit: i64,\n        offset: i64,\n    ) -> Result<Vec<DomainEvent>>;\n}\n\n/// Postgres implementation utilising `jsonb` column for payload + metadata.\npub struct PostgresEventStore {\n    pool: PgPool,\n}\n\nimpl PostgresEventStore {\n    pub fn new(pool: PgPool) -> Self {\n        Self { pool }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventStore for PostgresEventStore {\n    async fn append(\n        &self,\n        tx: &mut Transaction<'_, Postgres>,\n        event: &DomainEvent,\n    ) -> Result<()> {\n        let payload_json = serde_json::to_value(event)?;\n        sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO domain_events (\n                id,\n                occurred_at,\n                aggregate_id,\n                correlation_id,\n                causation_id,\n                actor_id,\n                schema_version,\n                payload\n            )\n            VALUES ($1,$2,$3,$4,$5,$6,$7,$8)\n            \"#,\n            event.id,\n            event.occurred_at,\n            extract_aggregate_id(event), // helper below\n            event.correlation_id,\n            event.causation_id,\n            event.actor_id,\n            event.schema_version as i32,\n            payload_json\n        )\n        .execute(&mut *tx)\n        .await\n        .context(\"failed to append domain event\")?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    async fn list_by_aggregate(\n        &self,\n        aggregate_id: Uuid,\n        limit: i64,\n        offset: i64,\n    ) -> Result<Vec<DomainEvent>> {\n        let rows = sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT payload\n            FROM domain_events\n            WHERE aggregate_id = $1\n            ORDER BY occurred_at\n            LIMIT $2 OFFSET $3\n            \"#,\n            aggregate_id,\n            limit,\n            offset\n        )\n        .fetch_all(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        rows.into_iter()\n            .map(|row| {\n                let value: serde_json::Value = row.payload;\n                let ev: DomainEvent = serde_json::from_value(value)\n                    .context(\"failed to deserialize stored event\")?;\n                Ok(ev)\n            })\n            .collect()\n    }\n}\n\n/// Extracts a stable aggregate identifier from the given event. For some events\n/// the aggregate is obvious (e.g., `lesson_id`), for others a different domain\n/// decision may be needed. For demo purposes we pick the first UUID.\nfn extract_aggregate_id(event: &DomainEvent) -> Uuid {\n    match &event.payload {\n        DomainEventKind::LessonPublished { lesson_id, .. } => *lesson_id,\n        DomainEventKind::QuizSubmitted { quiz_id, .. } => *quiz_id,\n        DomainEventKind::BadgeAwarded { badge_id, .. } => *badge_id,\n        DomainEventKind::PaymentProcessed { payment_id, .. } => *payment_id,\n        DomainEventKind::LearningPulseCreated { pulse_id, .. } => *pulse_id,\n        DomainEventKind::LearningPulseResponded { pulse_id, .. } => *pulse_id,\n    }\n}\n\n// -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// LearningPulse Service (Use-Case Example)\n// -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// DTO used by controllers when a teacher creates a new learning pulse.\n#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]\npub struct NewLearningPulse {\n    pub teacher_id: Uuid,\n    pub title: String,\n    pub description: Option<String>,\n    pub due_date: Option<DateTime<Utc>>,\n}\n\n/// Lightweight representation of a persisted learning pulse.\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize)]\npub struct LearningPulse {\n    pub id: Uuid,\n    pub teacher_id: Uuid,\n    pub title: String,\n    pub description: Option<String>,\n    pub due_date: Option<DateTime<Utc>>,\n    pub created_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n}\n\n/// Service encapsulating the invariants and business rules related to Learning\n/// Pulses. Other bounded contexts interact via REST or gRPC, never directly\n/// with repositories.\npub struct LearningPulseService {\n    pool: PgPool,\n    event_store: Arc<dyn EventStore>,\n    publisher: Arc<dyn EventPublisher>,\n}\n\nimpl LearningPulseService {\n    pub fn new(\n        pool: PgPool,\n        event_store: Arc<dyn EventStore>,\n        publisher: Arc<dyn EventPublisher>,\n    ) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            pool,\n            event_store,\n            publisher,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Creates a new learning pulse in a single ACID transaction, appends and\n    /// publishes the corresponding domain event only **after** the DB write\n    /// succeeds (`outbox` pattern).\n    #[instrument(skip(self))]\n    pub async fn create_pulse(&self, dto: NewLearningPulse) -> Result<LearningPulse> {\n        // Begin transaction.\n        let mut tx = self.pool.begin().await?;\n        let pulse_id = Uuid::new_v4();\n\n        // Insert row.\n        let q: PgQueryResult = sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO learning_pulses (id, teacher_id, title, description, due_date)\n            VALUES ($1,$2,$3,$4,$5)\n            \"#,\n            pulse_id,\n            dto.teacher_id,\n            dto.title,\n            dto.description,\n            dto.due_date\n        )\n        .execute(&mut *tx)\n        .await\n        .context(\"failed to insert learning pulse\")?;\n\n        debug!(rows_affected = q.rows_affected(), \"inserted learning pulse\");\n\n        // Build domain event.\n        let event = DomainEvent::new(\n            Some(dto.teacher_id),\n            DomainEventKind::LearningPulseCreated {\n                pulse_id,\n                teacher_id: dto.teacher_id,\n            },\n        );\n\n        // Persist event to store.\n        self.event_store.append(&mut tx, &event).await?;\n\n        // Commit.\n        tx.commit().await?;\n\n        // Publish *after* commit to ensure read-your-own writes semantics.\n        if let Err(e) = self.publisher.publish(&event).await {\n            // Publishing failures should be retried via a dead-letter queue in\n            // real deployments. remains agnostic of the transport layer.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventPublisher: Send + Sync {\n    /// Asynchronously publish a single event.\n    async fn publish(&self, envelope: &EventEnvelope) -> Result<()>;\n}\n\n/// RabbitMQ implementation of `EventPublisher`.\n///\n/// It relies on:\n///  - AMQP 0-9-1 protocol with publisher confirmations\n///  - JSON-serialised payloads\npub struct RabbitPublisher {\n    channel: Channel,\n    exchange: String,\n}\n\nimpl RabbitPublisher {\n    /// Establish the underlying AMQP connection and create a `RabbitPublisher`.\n    pub async fn connect(uri: &str, exchange: &str) -> Result<Arc<Self>> {\n        info!(\"Connecting to RabbitMQ at {uri}\");\n        let conn_props = ConnectionProperties::default().with_default_executor(8);\n        let conn = Connection::connect(uri, conn_props)\n            .await\n            .with_context(|| \"Failed to connect to RabbitMQ\")?;\n\n        let channel = conn\n            .create_channel()\n            .await\n            .with_context(|| \"Opening channel\")?;\n\n        channel\n            .confirm_select(ConfirmSelectOptions { nowait: false })\n            .await\n            .with_context(|| \"Enabling publisher confirms\")?;\n\n        Ok(Arc::new(Self {\n            channel,\n            exchange: exchange.to_owned(),\n        }))\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl .with_message_id(envelope.id.to_string().into()),\n            )\n            .await?\n            .await\n            .context(\"Awaiting broker confirmation\")?;\n\n        if confirm.is_nack() {\n convenient helpers.\n///\n/// Consumers of the service layer can remain transport-agnostic and\n/// inject any `EventPublisher` implementation.\npub struct PulseService<P: EventPublisher> {\n {pulse_id} by student {student_id}\"\n        );\n\n        let event = EventEnvelope::new(Event::SubmissionReceived(SubmissionReceived {\n            submission_id,\n            pulse_id,\n Using a single error enum\n/// (rather than scattering opaque `anyhow::Error`s) makes pattern-matching\n/// in callers easier and metrics aggregation cleaner.\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum EventStoreError {\n    #[error(\"database connection error: {0:?}\")]\n    Connection(#[from] sqlx::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"serialization error: {0:?}\")]\n    Serde(#[from] serde_json::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"attempt to erase non-existing user {0}\")]\n    UnknownUser(Uuid),\n\n    #[error(\"unexpected error: {0}\")]\n    Other(#[from] anyhow::Error),\n}\n\n/// Abstraction for writing/reading events.  The trait is intentionally narrow\n/// so that adapters for DynamoDB, Kafka-compacted topics, or filesystem\n/// snapshots can be added later with minimal friction.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventStore: Send + Sync {\n    async fn append_event(&self, event: &AuditEvent) -> Result<(), EventStoreError>;\n    async fn fetch_events_by_aggregate(\n        &self,\n        aggregate_id: Uuid,\n    ) -> Result<Vec<AuditEvent>, EventStoreError>;\n    /// GDPR-style erase.  The default behaviour is pseudonymisation:\n    /// personal fields in the JSON payload are stripped/replaced while\n    /// keeping aggregate-level metadata for analytics/regulatory audits.\n    async fn pseudonymise_user(\n        &self,\n        user_id: Uuid,\n        pseudonym: &str,\n    ) -> Result<(), EventStoreError>;\n}\n\n/// Postgres 15 implementation.  Requires the following DDL:\n///\n/// ```sql\n/// CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS audit_events (\n///     event_id     UUID PRIMARY KEY,\n///     aggregate_id UUID NOT NULL,\n///     event_type   VARCHAR NOT NULL,\n///     payload      JSONB NOT NULL,\n///     created_at   TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()\n/// );\n/// CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_audit_aggregate\n///     ON audit_events (aggregate_id);\n/// CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_audit_event_type\n///     ON audit_events (event_type);\n/// ```\npub struct PgEventStore {\n    pool: PgPool,\n}\n\nimpl PgEventStore {\n    /// Reads standard environment variables (e.g., `DATABASE_URL`) if `pool`\n    /// is not supplied.  Callers can also pass a pre-initialised `PgPool`\n    /// obtained from an application-wide connection manager.\n    pub async fn new(pool: Option<PgPool>) -> Result<Self, EventStoreError> {\n        match pool {\n            Some(p) => Ok(Self { pool: p }),\n            None => {\n                let db_url = env::var(\"DATABASE_URL\")\n                    .map_err(|_| anyhow::anyhow!(\"DATABASE_URL env var not set\"))?;\n                let max_conns: u32 =\n                    env::var(\"DATABASE_MAX_CONNS\").unwrap_or_else(|_| \"8\".into()).parse().unwrap();\n                let pool = PgPool::builder()\n                    .max_size(max_conns)\n                    .build(&db_url)\n                    .await?;\n                Ok(Self { pool })\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventStore for PgEventStore {\n    #[instrument(skip_all, fields(event_id=%event.event_id))]\n    async fn append_event(&self, event: &AuditEvent) -> Result<(), EventStoreError> {\n        sqlx::query(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO audit_events (event_id, aggregate_id, event_type, payload, created_at)\n            VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5)\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(event.event_id)\n        .bind(event.aggregate_id)\n        .bind(event.event_type.clone())\n        .bind(&event.payload)\n        .bind(event.created_at)\n        .execute(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        info!(%event.event_id, \"event persisted\");\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    #[instrument(skip_all, fields(aggregate_id=%aggregate_id))]\n    async fn fetch_events_by_aggregate(\n        &self,\n        aggregate_id: Uuid,\n    ) -> Result<Vec<AuditEvent>, EventStoreError> {\n        let rows = sqlx::query(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT event_id, aggregate_id, event_type, payload, created_at\n            FROM audit_events\n            WHERE aggregate_id = $1\n            ORDER BY created_at ASC\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(aggregate_id)\n        .fetch_all(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        let events = rows.into_iter().map(row_to_event).collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;\n        Ok(events)\n    }\n\n    /// Iterates through *all* events where `aggregate_id = user_id`, then\n    /// transforms the JSON payload via `pseudonymise_payload`.  Runs inside a\n    /// `BEGIN … COMMIT` block to guarantee atomicity.\n    #[instrument(skip_all, fields(user_id=%user_id))]\n    async fn pseudonymise_user(\n        &self,\n        user_id: Uuid,\n        pseudonym: &str,\n    ) -> Result<(), EventStoreError> {\n        let mut tx = self.pool.begin().await?;\n\n        let affected: i64 = sqlx::query_scalar(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT COUNT(*) FROM audit_events\n            WHERE aggregate_id = $1\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(user_id)\n        .fetch_one(&mut *tx)\n        .await?;\n\n        if affected == 0 {\n            return Err(EventStoreError::UnknownUser(user_id));\n        }\n\n        // Fetch, transform, write back\n        let rows = sqlx::query(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT event_id, aggregate_id, event_type, payload, created_at\n            FROM audit_events\n            WHERE aggregate_id = $1\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(user_id)\n        .fetch_all(&mut *tx)\n        .await?;\n\n        for row in rows {\n            let mut event = row_to_event(row)?;\n            event.payload = pseudonymise_payload(&event.payload, pseudonym)?;\n            sqlx::query(\n                r#\"\n                UPDATE audit_events\n                SET payload = $1\n                WHERE event_id = $2\n                \"#,\n            )\n            .bind(&event.payload)\n            .bind(event.event_id)\n            .execute(&mut *tx)\n            .await?;\n        }\n\n        tx.commit().await?;\n        info!(%user_id, \"pseudonymisation complete ({} events)\", affected);\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/// Convert a database row into `AuditEvent`.\nfn row_to_event(row: PgRow) -> Result<AuditEvent, EventStoreError> {\n    Ok(AuditEvent {\n        event_id: row.try_get(\"event_id\")?,\n        aggregate_id: row.try_get(\"aggregate_id\")?,\n        event_type: row.try_get(\"event_type\")?,\n        payload: row.try_get(\"payload\")?,\n        created_at: row.try_get(\"created_at\")?,\n    })\n}\n\n/// Recurse through JSON to strip direct identifiers (name, email, …) and\n/// replace them with a constant pseudonym.  This is obviously simplistic,\n/// but demonstrates the technique.  Real-world code would leverage schema\n/// introspection or build explicit per-event transformers.\nfn pseudonymise_payload(\n    original: &JsonValue,\n    pseudonym: &str,\n) -> Result<JsonValue, EventStoreError> {\n    let mut clone = original.clone();\n\n    match &mut clone {\n        JsonValue::Object(map) => {\n            static SENSITIVE_KEYS: &[&str] = &[\"name\", \"email\", \"first_name\", \"last_name\"];\n            for key in SENSITIVE_KEYS {\n                if map.contains_key(*key) {\n                    map.insert((*key).to_string(), JsonValue::String(pseudonym.to_owned()));\n                }\n            }\n            // Recurse into nested values\n            for v in map.values_mut() {\n                *v = pseudonymise_payload(v, pseudonym)?;\n            }\n        }\n        JsonValue::Array(arr) => {\n            for v in arr.iter_mut() {\n                *v = pseudonymise_payload(v, pseudonym)?;\n            }\n        }\n        _ => { /* primitives stay unchanged */ }\n    }\n    Ok(clone)\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use serde_json::json;\n\n    #[tokio::test]\n    async fn test_pseudonymise_payload() {\n        let original = json!({\n            \"name\": \"Alice\",\n            \"email\": \"alice@example.com\",\n            \"score\": 95,\n            \"nested\": {\n                \"first_name\": \"Alice\",\n                \"comments\": [\"great job\"]\n            }\n        });\n\n        let transformed = pseudonymise_payload(&original, \"redacted\").unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(transformed[\"name\"], \"redacted\");\n        assert_eq!(transformed[\"nested\"][\"first_name\"], \"redacted\");\n        assert_eq!(transformed[\"score\"], 95); // untouched\n    }\n\n    #[tokio::test]\n    async fn test_append_and_fetch_roundtrip() -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n        // Spin up a transient in-memory Postgres in CI or use `TEST_DATABASE_URL`.\n        let db_url =\n            std::env::var(\"TEST_DATABASE_URL\").unwrap_or_else(|_| \"postgres://postgres@localhost/ep_test\".into());\n        let pool = PgPool::connect(&db_url).await?;\n        // Ensure table exists for the test\n        sqlx::query(\n            r#\"\n            CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS audit_events (\n                event_id     UUID PRIMARY KEY,\n                aggregate_id UUID NOT NULL,\n                event_type   VARCHAR NOT NULL,\n                payload      JSONB NOT NULL,\n                created_at   TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL\n            );\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .execute(&pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        let store = PgEventStore::new(Some(pool.clone())).await?;\n\n        let event = AuditEvent {\n            event_id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            aggregate_id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            event_type: DomainEventType::LessonPublished,\n            payload: json!({\"lesson_id\": 42, \"title\": \"Rust Ownership\"}),\n            created_at: Utc::now(),\n        };\n\n        store.append_event(&event).await?;\n        let fetched = store.fetch_events_by_aggregate(event.aggregate_id).await?;\n        assert_eq!(fetched.len(), 1);\n        assert_eq!(fetched[0].payload[\"lesson_id\"], 42);\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n```","numLines":373,"startLine":1,"totalLines":373}}}

sync::Arc,\n    time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH},\n};\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse thiserror::Error;\nuse tokio::{\n    sync::{broadcast, Mutex},\n    task::JoinHandle,\n};\nuse tracing::{debug, error, info};\nuse uuid::Uuid;\n\n/// Human-readable routing key.\npub type Topic = String;\n\n/// Errors produced by the broker layer.\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum BrokerError {\n    #[error(\"message serialization failed: {0}\")]\n    Serialization(#[from] serde_json::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"channel error: {0}\")]\n    Channel(#[from] broadcast::error::RecvError),\n\n    #[error(\"topic not found: {0}\")]\n    TopicNotFound(String),\n\n    #[error(\"unknown broker error: {0}\")]\n    Unknown(String),\n}\n\n/// Envelope that wraps every domain event.\n///\n/// Adds metadata such as a unique id and timestamp.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct EventEnvelope<T>\nwhere\n    T: Serialize + Send + Sync + 'static,\n{\n    /// Globally unique event identifier.\n    pub id: Uuid,\n\n    /// Logical routing key.\n    pub topic: Topic,\n\n    /// Milliseconds since UNIX_EPOCH.\n    pub ts_millis: u128,\n\n    /// Optional correlation id to track causality chains.\n    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = \"Option::is_none\")]\n    pub correlation_id: Option<Uuid>,\n\n    /// Actual domain payload.\n    pub payload: T,\n}\n\nimpl<T> EventEnvelope<T>\nwhere\n    T: Serialize + Send + Sync + 'static,\n{\n    /// Create a new envelope with the current system time.\n    pub fn new(topic: impl Into<Topic>, payload: T) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            topic: topic.into(),\n            ts_millis: SystemTime::now()\n                .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)\n                .expect(\"system clock drift\")\n                .as_millis(),\n            correlation_id: None,\n            payload,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Attach or overwrite the correlation id.\n    pub fn with_correlation_id(mut self, id: Uuid) -> Self {\n        self.correlation_id = Some(id);\n        self\n    }\n}\n\n/// High-level abstraction over different message brokers.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventBroker: Send + Sync {\n    /// Publish a domain event.\n    async fn publish<T>(&self, event: EventEnvelope<T>) -> Result<(), BrokerError>\n    where\n        T: Serialize + Send + Sync + 'static;\n\n    /// Subscribe to a topic, returning a stream-like handle.\n    async fn subscribe(&self, topic: Topic) -> Result<Subscription, BrokerError>;\n\n    /// Gracefully stop internal background tasks.\n    async fn stop(&self) -> Result<(), BrokerError>;\n}\n\n/// Stream-like subscription that yields raw JSON bytes.\n///\n/// The consumer decides how to deserialize them.\n#[derive(Debug)]\npub struct Subscription {\n    topic: Topic,\n    rx: broadcast::Receiver<Vec<u8>>,\n}\n\nimpl Subscription {\n    pub fn topic(&self) -> &str {\n        &self.topic\n    }\n\n    pub async fn recv(&mut self) -> Result<Vec<u8>, BrokerError> {\n        let bytes = self.rx.recv().await?;\n        Ok(bytes)\n    }\n}\n\n/// In-memory broker backed by Tokio broadcast channels.\n///\n/// Suitable for unit tests and local development but **not** for production.\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct InMemoryBroker {\n    /// Mapping topic ⇒ channel sender.\n    inner: Arc<Mutex<HashMap<Topic, broadcast::Sender<Vec<u8>>>>>,\n    /// Background tasks (reserved for future use).\n    tasks: Arc<Mutex<Vec<JoinHandle<()>>>>,\n}\n\nimpl InMemoryBroker {\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            inner: Arc::new(Mutex::new(HashMap::new())),\n            tasks: Arc::new(Mutex::new(Vec::new())),\n        }\n    }\n\n    async fn get_sender(&self, topic: &Topic) -> broadcast::Sender<Vec<u8>> {\n        let mut guard = self.inner.lock().await;\n        guard\n            .entry(topic.clone())\n            .or_insert_with(|| broadcast::channel::<Vec<u8>>(1024).0)\n            .clone()\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventBroker for InMemoryBroker {\n    async fn publish<T>(&self, event: EventEnvelope<T>) -> Result<(), BrokerError>\n    where\n        T: Serialize + Send + Sync + 'static,\n    {\n        let bytes = serde_json::to_vec(&event)?;\n        let sender = self.get_sender(&event.topic).await;\n\n        match sender.send(bytes) {\n            Ok(receivers) => {\n                debug!(\n                    \"published event {} on '{}' ({} receivers)\",\n                    event.id, event.topic, receivers\n                );\n                Ok(())\n            }\n            Err(e) => {\n                error!(\"publish failed: {e}\");\n                Err(BrokerError::Unknown(e.to_string()))\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    async fn subscribe(&self, topic: Topic) -> Result<Subscription, BrokerError> {\n        let sender = self.get_sender(&topic).await;\n        Ok(Subscription {\n            topic,\n            rx: sender.subscribe(),\n        })\n    }\n\n    async fn stop(&self) -> Result<(), BrokerError> {\n        let mut tasks = self.tasks.lock().await;\n        for handle in tasks.drain(..) {\n            handle.abort();\n        }\n        info!(\"in-memory broker stopped\");\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/// Example domain events.\n\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct LessonPublished {\n    pub lesson_id: Uuid,\n    pub author_id: Uuid,\n    pub title: String,\n}\n\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct QuizSubmitted {\n    pub quiz_id: Uuid,\n    pub student_id: Uuid,\n    pub score: f32,\n}\n\n/// High-level typed facade used by application services.\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct EventBus {\n    broker: Arc<dyn EventBroker>,\n}\n\nimpl EventBus {\n    pub fn new(broker: Arc<dyn EventBroker>) -> Self {\n        Self { broker }\n    }\n\n    /// Generic emit helper.\n    pub async fn emit<T>(&self, topic: impl Into<Topic>, payload: T) -> Result<(), BrokerError>\n    where\n        T: Serialize + Send + Sync + 'static,\n    {\n        let env = EventEnvelope::new(topic, payload);\n        self.broker.publish(env).await\n    }\n\n    // --------------------------------------------------\n    // Convenience wrappers for known domain events.\n    // --------------------------------------------------\n\n    pub async fn lesson_published(&self, e: LessonPublished) -> Result<(), BrokerError> {\n        self.emit(\"learning.lesson.published\", e).await\n    }\n\n    pub async fn quiz_submitted(&self, e: QuizSubmitted) -> Result<(), BrokerError> {\n        self.emit(\"learning.quiz.submitted\", e).await\n    }\n\n    // --------------------------------------------------\n    // Typed subscriptions\n    // --------------------------------------------------\n\n    pub async fn on_lesson_published(\n        &self,\n    ) -> Result<TypedSubscription<LessonPublished>, BrokerError> {\n        let raw = self.broker.subscribe(\"learning.lesson.published\".into()).await?;\n        Ok(TypedSubscription::from_raw(raw))\n    }\n\n    pub async fn on_quiz_submitted(\n        &self,\n    ) -> Result<TypedSubscription<QuizSubmitted>, BrokerError> {\n        let raw = self.broker.subscribe(\"learning.quiz.submitted\".into()).await?;\n        Ok(TypedSubscription::from_raw(raw))\n    }\n}\n\n/// Strongly-typed subscription wrapper.\npub struct TypedSubscription<T>\nwhere\n    T: for<'de> Deserialize<'de> + Send + 'static,\n{\n    inner: Subscription,\n    _phantom: std::marker::PhantomData<T>,\n}\n\nimpl<T> TypedSubscription<T>\nwhere\n    T: for<'de> Deserialize<'de> + Send + 'static,\n{\n    fn from_raw(inner: Subscription) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            inner,\n            _phantom: Default::default(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    pub async fn recv(&mut self) -> Result<T, BrokerError> {\n        let bytes = self.inner.recv().await?;\n        let envelope: EventEnvelope<T> = serde_json::from_slice(&bytes)?;\n        Ok(envelope.payload)\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use tokio::time::{timeout, Duration};\n\n    #[tokio::test]\n    async fn roundtrip_lesson_published() {\n        let broker = Arc::new(InMemoryBroker::new());\n        let bus = EventBus::new(broker);\n\n        let mut sub = bus.on_lesson_published().await.unwrap();\n\n        let evt = LessonPublished {\n            lesson_id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            author_id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            title: \"Advanced Rust Lifetimes\".into(),\n        };\n\n        bus.lesson_published(evt.clone()).await.unwrap();\n\n        let received = timeout(Duration::from_secs(1), sub.recv())\n            .await\n            .expect(\"timed-out\")\n            .unwrap();\n\n        assert_eq!(evt.lesson_id, received.lesson_id);\n        assert_eq!(evt.title, received.title);\n    }\n}\n```","numLines":322,"startLine":1,"totalLines":322}}}

An event–driven badge–awarding micro-service for the\n//! “EduPulse Live – Event-Driven Social Learning Hub” platform.\n//!\n//! This module demonstrates how the core application publishes\n//! domain events while an out-of-process, autonomous component\n//! (here: `BadgeAwardService`) consumes them, performs business\n//! logic, persists the result and publishes its own follow-up\n//! events.  \n//!\n//! The service is completely self-contained: it hosts an in-memory\n//! dispatcher for local development, yet persists events and state\n//! in Postgres so it can be moved into a distributed setting\n//! later on (e.g. with NATS/Kafka‐backed `Dispatcher`).\n//!\n//! Architectural patterns shown here:\n//!   • Repository Pattern      – decoupling persistence\n//!   • Service Layer           – `BadgeAwardService`\n//!   • Event Sourcing          – immutable `EventStore`\n//!   • Async Rust w/ Tokio     – non-blocking I/O\n//!   • Error Handling w/ thiserror + anyhow\n//!\n//! NOTE: In a real-world project this module would be split\n//! into several files/crates.  Everything is co-located here only\n//! to satisfy the single-file constraint of the exercise.\n\nuse std::{\n    collections::HashSet,\n    sync::Arc,\n    time::Duration,\n};\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse sqlx::{postgres::PgPoolOptions, PgPool};\nuse thiserror::Error;\nuse tokio::{\n    select,\n    sync::{broadcast, mpsc, RwLock},\n    task::JoinHandle,\n};\nuse uuid::Uuid;\n\n/// Domain events which can be emitted by EduPulse services.\n///\n/// IMPORTANT: New variants **must** implement `Serialize`/`Deserialize`\n/// so they can be persisted in the event store.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(tag = \"type\", content = \"payload\")]\npub enum DomainEvent {\n    QuizSubmitted(QuizSubmitted),\n    BadgeAwarded(BadgeAwarded),\n    // … more variants omitted for brevity\n}\n\nimpl DomainEvent {\n    /// Helper returning event name for logging / metrics.\n    pub fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        match self {\n            DomainEvent::QuizSubmitted(_) => \"QuizSubmitted\",\n            DomainEvent::BadgeAwarded(_) => \"BadgeAwarded\",\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Metadata common to all envelopes—separates immutable event data\n/// from peripheral transport concerns such as IDs and timestamps.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct EventEnvelope {\n    pub id: Uuid,\n    pub occurred_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub event: DomainEvent,\n}\n\nimpl EventEnvelope {\n    pub fn new(event: DomainEvent) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            occurred_at: Utc::now(),\n            event,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Event data triggered when a learner submits a quiz.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, badge.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct BadgeAwarded {\n    pub badge_id: Uuid,\n    pub learner_id: Uuid,\n    pub awarded_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub reason: might replace the implementation with NATS,\n/// Kafka, RabbitMQ… etc., leaving the public API intact.\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct Dispatcher {\n    sender: broadcast::Sender<EventEnvelope>,\n}\n\nimpl Dispatcher {\n    /// Create a new dispatcher with a configurable ring buffer size.\n    pub fn new(channel_size: usize) -> Self {\n        let (sender, _rx) = broadcast::channel(channel_size);\n        Self { sender }\n    }\n\n    /// Publish an envelope to all subscribers.\n    pub async fn publish(&self, envelope: EventEnvelope) {\n        // broadcast::Sender::send is synchronous but inexpensive.\n        // Failure only occurs when there are no active receivers;\n        // still, we ignore that because the event is persisted in\n        // the store before reaching this point.\n        let _ = self.sender.send(envelope);\n    }\n\n    /// Subscribe to a continuous stream of events.\n    pub fn subscribe(&self) -> broadcast::Receiver<EventEnvelope> {\n        self.sender.subscribe()\n    }\n}\n\n/// Persisted event stream; provides idempotent append + read access.\n/// Uses Postgres JSONB for flexible schema evolution.\n///\n/// Table DDL (run via migrations):\n/// ```sql\n/// CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS event_store (\n///     id           UUID PRIMARY KEY,\n///     occurred_at  TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE NOT NULL,\n///     event_name   TEXT NOT NULL,\n///     payload      JSONB NOT NULL\n/// );\n/// ```\npub struct EventStore {\n    pool: PgPool,\n}\n\nimpl EventStore {\n    pub async fn new(db_url: &str, max_connections: u32) -> Result<Self, sqlx::Error> {\n        let pool = PgPoolOptions::new()\n            .max_connections(max_connections)\n            .connect(db_url)\n            .await?;\n        Ok(Self { pool })\n    }\n\n    /// Append envelope to store.\n    pub async fn append(&self, env: &EventEnvelope) -> Result<(), sqlx::Error> {\n        sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO event_store (id, occurred_at, event_name, payload)\n            VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4)\n            \"#,\n            env.id,\n            env.occurred_at,\n read models.\n    pub async fn load_by_event(\n        &self,\n        event_name: &str,\n    ) -> Result<Vec<EventEnvelope>, sqlx::Error> {\n        let recs = sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT id, occurred_at, payload\n            FROM event_store\n            WHERE event_name = $1\n            ORDER BY occurred_at ASC\n            \"#,\n            event_name\n        )\n        .fetch_all(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        recs.into_iter()\n            .map(|r| {\n                let event = match event_name {\n                    \"QuizSubmitted\" => DomainEvent::QuizSubmitted(\n                        serde_json::from_value(r.payload)?,\n                    ),\n                    \"BadgeAwarded\" => DomainEvent::BadgeAwarded(\n                        serde_json::from_value(r.payload)?,\n                    ),\n                    _ => unreachable!(\"Unknown event stored in DB\"),\n                };\n                Ok(EventEnvelope {\n                    id: r.id,\n                    occurred_at: r.occurred_at,\n                    event,\n                })\n            })\n            .collect()\n    }\n}\n\n/// Error type covering all repository/service error cases.\n#[derive(Error, Debug)]\npub enum ServiceError {\n    #[error(\"Database error: {0}\")]\n    Database(#[from] sqlx::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"Event serialisation error: {0}\")]\n    Serde(#[from] serde_json::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"Internal channel closed\")]\n    ChannelClosed,\n}\n\n/// Repository abstraction for badge persistence.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait BadgeRepository: Send + Sync {\n    async fn learner_already_has_badge(\n        &self,\n        learner_id: Uuid,\n        badge_id: Uuid,\n    ) -> Result<bool, ServiceError>;\n\n    async fn learner_id: Uuid,\n        badge_id: Uuid,\n    ) -> Result<bool, ServiceError> {\n        let res = sqlx::query_scalar!(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT EXISTS (\n                SELECT 1\n                FROM learner_badges\n                WHERE learner_id = $1 AND badge_id = $2\n            ) AS \"exists!\"\n            \"#,\n            learner_id,\n            badge_id\n        )\n        .fetch_one(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        Ok(res.unwrap_or(false))\n    }\n\n    async fn assign_badge(\n        &self,\n        learner_id: Uuid,\n        badge: &BadgeAwarded,\n    ) -> Result<(), ServiceError> {\n        sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO learner_badges (learner_id, badge_id, awarded_at, reason)\n            VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4)\n            \"#,\n            learner_id,\n            badge.badge_id,\n            badge.awarded_at,\n            badge.reason\n        )\n        .execute(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/// Business logic deciding whether a quiz submission qualifies\n/// for a badge.  Runs fully asynchronously, keeping its own\n/// subscription to the dispatcher.\n///\n/// Scoring rules used here are intentionally simple:  \n/// – Score ≥ 90 %      ⇒ “Quiz Master” badge (UUID hard-coded)  \n/// – Score exactly 100 ⇒ special “Perfect Score” badge\npub struct BadgeAwardService<R: BadgeRepository + 'static> {\n    repo: Arc<R>,\n    dispatcher: Dispatcher,\n    event_store: Arc<EventStore>,\n    subscribed_badges: HashSet<Uuid>,\n}\n\nimpl<R: BadgeRepository + 'static> BadgeAwardService<R> {\n    pub fn new(\n        repo: Arc<R>,\n        dispatcher: Dispatcher,\n        event_store: Arc<EventStore>,\n    ) -> Self {\n        // Hard-coded UUIDs of known badges.\n        let subscribed_badges = vec![\n            // “Quiz Master”\n            Uuid::parse_str(\"11111111-1111-1111-1111-111111111111\").unwrap(),\n            // “Perfect Score”\n            Uuid::parse_str(\"22222222-2222-2222-2222-222222222222\").unwrap(),\n        ]\n        .into_iter()\n        .collect();\n\n        Self {\n            repo,\n            dispatcher,\n            event_store,\n            subscribed_badges,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Spawn the service on the Tokio runtime; returns its `JoinHandle`\n    /// so callers can `.await` graceful shutdown.\n    pub fn spawn(self: Arc<Self>) -> JoinHandle<()> {\n        let mut rx = self.dispatcher.subscribe();\n\n        tokio::spawn(async move {\n            loop {\n                select! {\n                    biased;\n\n                    // React to new events\n                    msg = rx.recv() => match msg {\n                        Ok(env) => {\n                            if let Err(e) = self.process_event(env).await {\n                                tracing::error!(\"BadgeAwardService failed: {e}\");\n                            }\n                        },\n                        Err(broadcast::error::RecvError::Closed) => {\n                            tracing::warn!(\"Dispatcher closed, BadgeAwardService exiting.\");\n                            break;\n                        },\n                        Err(broadcast::error::RecvError::Lagged(skipped)) => {\n                            tracing::warn!(\"BadgeAwardService lagged, skipped {skipped} messages\");\n                        },\n                    },\n\n                    // Allow cooperative cancellation.\n                    _ = tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_secs(60)) => {},\n                }\n            }\n        })\n    }\n\n    async fn process_event(&self, env: EventEnvelope) -> Result<(), ServiceError> {\n        match env.event {\n            DomainEvent::QuizSubmitted(sub) => self.maybe_award_badge(sub).await?,\n            _ => { /* ignore */ }\n        }\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    async fn maybe_award_badge(&self, sub: QuizSubmitted) -> Result<(), ServiceError> {\n        // Determine qualifying badge.\n        let (badge_id, reason) = if (sub.score - 100.0).abs() < f32::EPSILON {\n            (\n                Uuid::parse_str(\"22222222-2222-2222-2222-222222222222\").unwrap(),\n                \"Perfect quiz score!\".to_owned(),\n            )\n        } else if sub.score >= 90.0 {\n            (\n                Uuid::parse_str(\"11111111-1111-1111-1111-111111111111\").unwrap(),\n                \"Scored 90% or above on a quiz.\".to_owned(),\n            )\n        } else {\n            return Ok(()); // Not eligible\n        };\n\n        // Filter unknown badge IDs (forward-compat).\n        if !self.subscribed_badges.contains(&badge_id) {\n            return Ok(());\n        }\n\n        // Idempotency check: learner may resubmit quiz multiple times.\n        if self\n            .repo\n            .learner_already_has_badge(sub.learner_id, badge_id)\n            .await?\n        {\n            return Ok(());\n        }\n\n        // Compose and persist badge award.\n on a rubric repository.\n    async fn auto_score(&self, submission: &SubmissionCreated) -> Result<u8, EventError> {\n        let rubric = self.rubric_store.fetch_current().await;\n        let words = submission.content.split_whitespace().count() as u32;\n        let mut score = 0;\n\n        if words >= rubric.min_word_count {\n            score += 60;\n        }\n        if submission.content.contains(\"because\") {\n            score += 15;\n        }\n        if submission.content.contains(\"therefore\") {\n            score += 15;\n        }\n        if submission.content.ends_with('.') {\n            score += 10;\n        }\n        Ok(score.min(100) as u8)\n    }\n}\n\n/// DTO returned from the remote plagiarism service.\n#[derive(Debug, }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Simplistic rubric repository.\n/// In production this would talk to Postgres or Config Service.\n#[derive(Default)]\npub struct RubricRepository {\n    current: .send(event.clone())\n            .map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!(e.to_string()))?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/* ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── Tests ─────────────── This module encapsulates everything required to reliably publish domain\n     7→//! events to an external broker while guaranteeing atomic persistence of the\n     8→//! associated business transaction (Transactional Outbox pattern).\n     9→//!\n    10→//! A high-level overview of the workflow:\n    11→//!\n    12→//! 1.  Business service saves its state changes inside a SQL transaction.\n    13→//! 2. 35→pub static NATS_SUBJECT: &str = \"edupulse.domain.events\";\n    36→\n    37→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    38→/// Domain Serialize + DeserializeOwned + fmt::Debug + Send + Sync {\n    46→    /// A stable string identifier (e.g. `learning_pulse.created`).\n    47→    fn event_type(&self) -> &'static str;\n    48→    /// DomainEvent> {\n    58→    pub event_id: uuid::Uuid,\n    59→    pub event_type: &'static str,\n    60→    pub version: &'static str,\n    61→    pub occurred_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    62→    pub Event Dispatcher & Outbox processor for EduPulse Live.\n//!\n//! This module encapsulates everything required to reliably publish domain\n//! events to an external broker while guaranteeing atomic persistence of the\n//! associated business transaction (Transactional Outbox pattern).\n//!\n//! A high-level overview of the workflow:\n//!\n//! 1.  Business service saves its state changes inside a SQL transaction.\n//! 2.  The same transaction stores an `OutboxEvent` row\n//!     inside `event_outbox` (JSONB payload).\n//! 3.  The [`OutboxProcessor`] picks up un-published events in batches,\n//!     publishes them to NATS, and marks them as dispatched.\n//!\n//! The implementation is `async`/`await` based and leverages\n//! industry-grade crates such as `sqlx`, `async-nats`, and `tracing`.\n\nuse std::{fmt, time::Duration};\n\nuse async_nats::{jetstream, Client as NatsClient, ConnectOptions};\nuse chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\nuse serde::{de::DeserializeOwned, Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse sqlx::{PgPool, Postgres, Transaction};\nuse thiserror::Error;\nuse tokio::time::sleep;\nuse tracing::{debug, error, info, instrument};\n\n/// The global NATS subject for EduPulse Live domain events.\n///\n/// Each microservice subscribes using its own durable consumer\n/// and optionally leverages JetStream for at-least-once delivery guarantees.\npub static NATS_SUBJECT: &str = \"edupulse.domain.events\";\n\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n/// Domain Event abstraction\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// Every domain event must implement this marker trait.\n///\n/// The trait bounds guarantee that all events are serialisable and debuggable,\n/// which enables structured logging & telemetry.\npub trait DomainEvent: Serialize + DeserializeOwned + fmt::Debug + Send + Sync {\n    /// A stable str;\n}\n\n/// Envelope for all outbound events.\n///\n/// It contains generic metadata required by consumers to perform\n/// deserialisation and correlation.\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct EventEnvelope<E: DomainEvent> {\n    pub event_id: uuid::Uuid,\n    pub event_type: &'static str,\n    pub version: &'static str,\n    pub occurred_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub aggregate_id: uuid::Uuid,\n    pub payload: E,\n}\n\n/// Helper to wrap a concrete event instance.\npub fn wrap_event<E: DomainEvent>(\n    aggregate_id: uuid::Uuid,\n    payload: E,\n) -> EventEnvelope<E> {\n    EventEnvelope {\n        event_id: uuid::Uuid::new_v4(),\n        event_type: payload.event_type(),\n        version: payload.version(),\n        occurred_at: Utc::now(),\n        aggregate_id,\n        payload,\n    }\n}\n\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n/// Outbox persistence\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// Row inside `event_outbox` table.\n///\n/// Inserted within the same DB transaction as the &EventEnvelope<E>,\n) -> Result<(), OutboxError>\nwhere\n    E: DomainEvent,\n{\n    let payload_json = serde_json::to_value(&envelope.payload)?;\n    sqlx::query!(\n        r#\"\n        INSERT INTO event_outbox \n            (aggregate_id, event_type, version, payload, occurred_at)\n        VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5)\n        \"#,\n        envelope.aggregate_id,\n        envelope.event_type,\n [`OutboxProcessor`].\n    pub async fn new(pg_pool: PgPool, nats_url: &str) -> Result<Self, OutboxError> {\n        let nats_client = NatsClient::connect_with_options(\n            nats_url,\n #[instrument(skip(self, shutdown), name = \"outbox.run\")]\n    pub async fn run_until_stopped(\n        &self,\n        poll_interval: Duration,\n        mut shutdown: tokio::sync::watch::Receiver<()>,\n    ) {\n        loop {\n            tokio::select! {\n                _ = shutdown.changed() => {\n                    info!(\"Outbox processor received shutdown   info!(\"Outbox processor terminated gracefully\");\n    }\n\n    /// Execute a single polling cycle.\n    #[instrument(skip(self))]\n    pub async fn tick(&self) {\n        match self.process_batch().await {\n            Ok(count) => {\n                if count > 0 {\n                    info!(count, \"outbox events dispatched\");\n                } else {\n                    debug!(\"no events to dispatch\");\n                }\n            }\n            Err(err) => {\n                error!(?err, \"failed to process outbox batch\");\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Fetch a batch of pending events, publish, and mark as dispatched.\n    #[instrument(skip(self), err)]\n    async fn process_batch(&self) -> Result<u64, OutboxError> {\n        let mut tx = self.pg_pool.begin().await?;\n\n        // Step 1: select for update – prevents concurrent processors\n        let events: Vec<OutboxEvent> = sqlx::query_as!(\n            OutboxEvent,\n            r#\"\n            SELECT * FROM event_outbox\n            WHERE published_at IS NULL\n            ORDER BY id\n            FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED\n            LIMIT $1\n            \"#,\n            BATCH_SIZE\n        )\n        .fetch_all(&mut tx)\n        .await?;\n\n        if events.is_empty() {\n            tx.rollback().await?;\n            return Ok(0);\n        }\n\n        // Step 2: publish each event to NATS\n        for evt in &events {\n            let payload_bytes = serde_json::to_vec(&evt)?;\n            self.nats\n                .publish(NATS_SUBJECT, payload_bytes.into())\n                .await?;\n        }\n\n        // Step 3: update published stamp\n        sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            UPDATE event_outbox\n            SET published_at = NOW()\n            WHERE id = ANY($1)\n            \"#,\n            &events.iter().map(|e| e.id).collect::<Vec<_>>()\n        )\n        .execute(&mut tx)\n        .await?;\n\n        tx.commit().await?;\n        Ok(events.len() as u64)\n    }\n}\n\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n/// Example domain event\n/// replaced at runtime\", handler.name());\n        } else {\n            info!(\"handler {} registered\", handler.name());\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Spawn a worker pool that fans-out events to
