This guarantees at-least-once delivery to\n    /// each handler.\n    pub fn start_worker_pool(self: &Arc<Self>, workers: usize) -> Vec<JoinHandle<()>> {\n        let mut handles = Vec::with_capacity(workers);\n        for id in 0..workers {\n            let (tx, mut rx) = mpsc::channel::<DomainEvent>(CHANNEL_CAPACITY);\n            self.worker_txs.push(tx);\n\n            let bus = Arc::clone(self);\n            let mut broadcast_rx = bus.tx.subscribe();\n            handles.push(spawn(async move {\n                info!(worker = id, \"event worker started\");\n                loop {\n                    select! {\n                        Ok(evt) = broadcast_rx.recv() => {\n                            // fan-in to single queue\n                            if let Err(e) = rx.try_send(evt) {\n                                debug!(%e, worker = id, \"worker queue full; dropping\");\n                            }\n                        },\n\n                        Some(evt) = rx.recv() => {\n                            for item in bus.handlers.iter() {\n                                let handler = item.value();\n                                let evt_clone = evt.clone();\n                                let h_name = handler.name();\n                                let res = handler.handle(evt_clone).await;\n                                if let Err(e) = res {\n                                    error!(%e, handler = h_name, \"event handler failed\");\n                                }\n                            }\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            }));\n        }\n        handles\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/* ----------------------- Example Production Handler ----------------------- */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// Tantivy-powered search indexer.\n///\n/// In a real deployment this would:\n/// 1. })\n    }\n\n    /// Append a *single* event to the store with OCC.\n    ///\n    /// Returns the stored [`EventEnvelope`] (with auto-generated id + timestamp).\n    #[instrument(skip(self, tx))]\n    pub async fn append_one(\n        &self,\n        mut tx: Transaction<'_, Postgres>,\n        aggregate_id: AggregateId,\n        expected_seq: i64,\n        event: DomainEvent,\n        correlation_id: Option<EventId>,\n    ) -> Result<EventEnvelope> {\n        // Next sequence number we intend to store.\n        let next_seq = expected_seq + 1;\n\n        let envelope = EventEnvelope::new(aggregate_id, next_seq, event, correlation_id);\n        let serialized = serde_json::to_value(&envelope)?;\n\n        let res = sqlx::query(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO event_store (id, aggregate_id, seq, payload)\n            VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4)\n            ON CONFLICT (aggregate_id, seq) DO NOTHING\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(envelope.id.0)\n        .bind(envelope.aggregate_id)\n        .bind(envelope.seq)\n        .bind(&serialized)\n        .execute(&mut *tx)\n        .await?;\n\n        if res.rows_affected() == 0 publisher }\n    }\n\n    /// Wrap a *business* operation in a transaction and publish all newly\n    /// appended events atomically (two-phase commit).\n    #[instrument(skip(self, op))]\n    pub async fn commit<F, Fut, T>(&self, op: F) -> Result<T>\n    where\n        F: FnOnce(&Self, &mut Transaction<'_, Postgres>) -> Fut + Send + Sync,\n        Fut: std::future::Future<Output = Result<T>> + Send,\n        T: Send,\n    {\n        let mut tx = self.store.begin().await?;\n\n        let result = op(self, &mut tx).await?;\n\n        // Extract fresh events written within the tx:\n        let Outbox/Inbox pattern for EduPulse Live.  All domain events are first\n     8→//! persisted inside the same Postgres transaction that modified the\n     9→//! aggregate and are later *atomically* published to the message broker\n    10→//! Reliable Domain Event Outbox implementation\n//! ===========================================\n//! This module provides a production-ready* implementation of the\n//! Outbox/Inbox pattern for EduPulse Live.  All domain events are first\n//! persisted inside the same Postgres transaction that modified the\n//! aggregate and are later *atomically* published to the message broker\n//! (e.g. RabbitMQ).  This guarantees *at-least-once* delivery without\n//! sacrificing database consistency.\n//!\n//! # Architecture\n//! 1. Domain layer inserts a row into the `outbox_events` table\n//!    through [`OutboxRepository::insert`].\n//! 2. [`OutboxProcessor`] polls the table on an interval (or is triggered\n//!    by NOTIFY/LISTEN) and publishes unpublished events.\n//! 3. After a successful publish it marks the event as `published_at`.\n//!\n//! # Usage\n//! ```no_run\n//! #[tokio::main]\n//! async fn main() -> anyhow::Result<()> {\n//!     env_logger::init();\n//!\n//!     let db = PgPoolOptions::new()\n//!         .max_connections(5)\n//!         .connect(\"postgres://user:password@localhost/edupulse\")\n//!         .await?;\n//!\n//!     // Initialise AMQP connection\n//!     let amqp_addr = \"amqp://guest:guest@127.0.0.1:5672/%2f\";\n//!     let publisher = RabbitMqPublisher::connect(amqp_addr, \"edupulse.events\").await?;\n//!\n//!     // Fire-and-forget background processing task\n//!     OutboxProcessor::spawn(db.clone(), publisher);\n//!\n//!     // … start REST API, etc.\n//!     Ok(())\n//! }\n//! ```\n//! *Error handling, retries, and back-off have been implemented but can be\n//! expanded with metrics, dead-letter queues, etc.*\n\nuse std::sync::Arc;\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse backoff::{future::retry, ExponentialBackoff};\nuse chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\nuse lapin::{\n    options::{BasicPublishOptions, QueueDeclareOptions},\n    types::FieldTable,\n    BasicProperties, Channel, Connection, ConnectionProperties,\n};\nuse log::{error, info, warn};\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse sqlx::{postgres::PgRow, PgPool, Row};\nuse thiserror::Error;\nuse tokio::{\n    sync::Mutex,\n    task::{self, JoinHandle},\n    time,\n};\nuse uuid::Uuid;\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                                Domain Event                                */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// A single row inside the `outbox_events` table.\n///\n/// NOTE: `payload` is stored as **raw JSON** to keep the table generic and\n/// future-proof.  Consumers can decode it based on the `event_type`.\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize, Clone)]\npub struct OutboxEvent {\n    pub id: Uuid,\n    pub aggregate_type: String,\n    pub aggregate_id: Uuid,\n    pub event_type: String,\n    pub payload: serde_json::Value,\n    pub occurred_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub published_at: Option<DateTime<Utc>>,\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                                  Errors                                    */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n#[derive(Error, Debug)]\npub enum OutboxError {\n    #[error(\"database error: {0}\")]\n    Database(#[from] sqlx::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"AMQP error: {0}\")]\n    Amqp(#[from] lapin::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"serialization error: {0}\")]\n    Serde(#[from] serde_json::Error),\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                           Outbox Repository (DAO)                          */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// Repository responsible for accessing the `outbox_events` table.\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct OutboxRepository {\n    pool: PgPool,\n}\n\nimpl OutboxRepository {\n    pub fn new(pool: PgPool) -> Self {\n        Self { pool }\n    }\n\n    /// Inserts a new [`OutboxEvent`] within an existing transaction or\n    /// standalone if `tx` is `None`.\n    pub async fn insert(\n        &self,\n        mut event: OutboxEvent,\n        tx: Option<&mut sqlx::Transaction<'_, sqlx::Postgres>>,\n    ) -> Result<OutboxEvent, OutboxError> {\n        event.id = event.id; // ensure id exists\n        let query = sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO outbox_events (\n                id,\n                aggregate_type,\n                aggregate_id,\n                event_type,\n                payload,\n                occurred_at,\n                published_at\n            ) VALUES ($1,$2,$3,$4,$5,$6,$7)\n            \"#,\n            event.id,\n            event.aggregate_type,\n            event.aggregate_id,\n            event.event_type,\n            event.payload,\n            event.occurred_at,\n            event.published_at\n        );\n\n        match tx {\n            Some(tx_ref) => {\n                query.execute(&mut *tx_ref).await?;\n            }\n            None => {\n                query.execute(&self.pool).await?;\n            }\n        }\n\n        Ok(event)\n    }\n\n    /// Fetches a batch of unpublished events, ordered by `occurred_at`.\n    pub async fn fetch_unpublished(\n        &self,\n        limit: i64,\n    ) -> Result<Vec<OutboxEvent>, OutboxError> {\n        let rows = sqlx::query(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT\n                id,\n                aggregate_type,\n                aggregate_id,\n                event_type,\n                payload,\n                occurred_at,\n                published_at\n            FROM outbox_events\n            WHERE published_at IS NULL\n            ORDER BY occurred_at\n            LIMIT $1\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(limit)\n        .map(|row: PgRow| OutboxEvent {\n            id: row.get(\"id\"),\n            aggregate_type: row.get(\"aggregate_type\"),\n            aggregate_id: row.get(\"aggregate_id\"),\n            event_type: row.get(\"event_type\"),\n            payload: row.get::<serde_json::Value, _>(\"payload\"),\n            occurred_at: row.get(\"occurred_at\"),\n            published_at: row.get(\"published_at\"),\n        })\n        .fetch_all(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        Ok(rows)\n    }\n\n    /// Marks the event as published.\n    pub async fn mark_as_published(&self, id: Uuid) -> Result<(), OutboxError> {\n        sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            UPDATE outbox_events\n            SET published_at = NOW()\n            WHERE id = $1\n            \"#,\n            id\n        )\n        .execute(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                             Event Publisher                                */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// Trait that abstracts away the underlying message broker implementation.\n/// This allows swapping RabbitMQ for NATS, Kafka, etc. without touching the\n/// domain/application layer.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventPublisher: Send + Sync + 'static {\n    async fn publish(&self, event: &OutboxEvent) -> Result<(), OutboxError>;\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                             RabbitMQ Publisher                             */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// Thin wrapper around a RabbitMQ channel that implements [`EventPublisher`].\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct RabbitMqPublisher {\n    channel: Arc<Channel>,\n    exchange: String,\n}\n\nimpl RabbitMqPublisher {\n    /// Establishes a connection and ensures that the declared exchange exists.\n    pub async fn connect(\n        amqp_addr: &str,\n        exchange: &str,\n    ) -> Result<Self, OutboxError> {\n        let conn = Connection::connect(amqp_addr, ConnectionProperties::default()).await?;\n        let channel = conn.create_channel().await?;\n\n        channel\n            .queue_declare(\n                exchange,\n                QueueDeclareOptions::default(),\n                FieldTable::default(),\n            )\n            .await?;\n\n        Ok(Self {\n            channel: Arc::new(channel),\n            exchange: exchange.to_string(),\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventPublisher for RabbitMqPublisher {\n    async fn publish(&self, event: &OutboxEvent) -> Result<(), OutboxError> {\n        let payload = serde_json::to_vec(event)?;\n        self.channel\n            .basic_publish(\n                \"\",\n                &self.exchange,\n                BasicPublishOptions::default(),\n                &payload,\n                BasicProperties::default()\n                    .with_content_type(\"application/json\".into())\n                    .with_message_id(event.id.to_string().into())\n                    .with_type(event.event_type.clone().into()),\n            )\n            .await?\n            .await?; // wait for broker confirmation\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                            Outbox Processor                                */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// Responsible for periodically polling the DB, publishing events and\n/// updating their status. It runs indefinitely in a tokio task.\npub struct OutboxProcessor {\n    handle: JoinHandle<()>,\n}\n\nimpl OutboxProcessor {\n    /// Spawns the background `OutboxProcessor` task with sensible defaults.\n    ///\n    /// `poll_interval` defaults to 500 ms and `batch_size` to 100.\n    pub fn spawn<P>(pool: PgPool, publisher: P) -> Self\n    where\n        P: EventPublisher,\n    {\n        let poll_interval = Duration::from_millis(500);\n        let batch_size = 100;\n\n        let repo = OutboxRepository::new(pool);\n        let publisher = Arc::new(publisher);\n\n        let handle = task::spawn(async move {\n            let mut interval = time::interval(poll_interval);\n            loop {\n                interval.tick().await;\n                if let Err(err) =\n                    Self::drain_batch(&repo, Arc::clone(&publisher), batch_size).await\n                {\n                    error!(\"Error draining outbox: {:?}\", err);\n                }\n            }\n        });\n\n        Self { handle }\n    }\n\n    async fn drain_batch<P>(\n        repo: &OutboxRepository,\n        publisher: Arc<P>,\n        batch_size: i64,\n    ) -> Result<(), OutboxError>\n    where\n        P: EventPublisher,\n    {\n        // Fetch a batch; early return if empty\n        let events = repo.fetch_unpublished(batch_size).await?;\n        if events.is_empty() {\n            return Ok(());\n        }\n\n        // Publish events sequentially (could be parallel with `join_all` if\n        // ordering isn't important)\n        for event in events {\n            let op = || async {\n                publisher.publish(&event).await?;\n                repo.mark_as_published(event.id).await?;\n                Ok::<(), OutboxError>(())\n            };\n\n            // Retry with exponential back-off (max ~10 min)\n            let mut backoff = ExponentialBackoff {\n                initial_interval: Duration::from_millis(250),\n                multiplier: 2.0,\n                max_interval: Duration::from_secs(30),\n                max_elapsed_time: Some(Duration::from_secs(600)),\n                ..Default::default()\n            };\n\n            if let Err(err) = retry(backoff, op).await {\n                // This should rarely happen; send to Sentry, Dead-letter, etc.\n                warn!(\"Failed to publish event {}: {:?}\", event.id, err);\n            }\n        }\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    /// Waits for the background task to finish (e.g. on graceful shutdown).\n    pub async fn join(self) -> Result<(), task::JoinError> {\n        self.handle.await\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                                   Tests                                    */\n/* 1;\n            Ok(())\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[sqlx::test(migrations = \"../migrations\")]\n    async fn processor_publishes_and_marks_event(pool: PgPool) -> Result<(), Box<dyn event = OutboxEvent {\n            id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            aggregate_type: \"Lesson\".into(),\n            aggregate_id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            event_type: \"LessonPublished\".into(),\n            payload: serde_json::json!({\"title\": \"Intro\"}),\n            occurred_at: now,\n            published_at: None,\n        };\n\n        repo.insert(event.clone(), None).await?;\n\n .context(\"decode jwt\")?;\n   124→    Ok(Uuid::parse_str(&data.claims.sub)?)\n   125→}\n   126→\n   127→// ---------- Domain Events ---------------------------------------------------\n   128→\n   129→#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n   130→pub enum DomainEvent {\n   131→    PulseCreated { pulse_id: Uuid, teacher_id: Uuid },\n   132→    // … more events (QuizSubmitted, BadgeGranted, token\"))?;\n   296→\n   297→    // Persist refreshed session (sliding expiration)\n   298→    session.renew();\n   299→\n   300→    let pulse = service\n   301→        .create_pulse(user_id, req.into_inner())\n   302→        .await\n   303→        .map_err(|e| {\n   304→            log::error!(\"create_pulse: {:?}\", e);\n   305→            actix_web::error::ErrorInternalServerError(\"failed to create pulse\")\n   306→        })?;\n   307→\n   308→    Ok(HttpResponse::Created().json(PulseResponse {\n   309→        id: pulse.id,\n   310→        title: pulse.title,\n   311→        description: pulse.description,\n   312→        created_at: pulse.created_at,\n   313→    }))\n   314→}\n   315→\n   316→#[get(\"/v1/pulses\")]\n   317→async Bootstrap (SSL + Middleware) ------------------------------------\n   353→\n   354→pub async fn build_server(\n   355→    db_url: &str,\n   356→    redis_url: &str,\n   357→    bind_addr: &str,\n   358→    tls_key: &str,\n   359→    tls_cert: &str,\n   360→) -> Result<Server> {\n   361→    // Logging\n   362→    env_logger::init_from_env(env_logger::Env::default().default_filter_or(\"info\"));\n .connect(db_url)\n   369→        .await?;\n   370→\n   371→    // Repositories & event publisher\n   372→    let repo = Arc::new(PgPulseRepository::new(db_pool)) as Arc<dyn PulseRepository>;\n   373→ .timeout(Duration::from_secs(2))\n   378→        .build()?;\n   379→\n   380→    // Service\n   381→    let service = PulseService::new(repo, events, search_http);\n   382→\n   383→    // JWT keys\n   384→    let secret = std::env::var(\"EP_JWT_SECRET\").unwrap_or_else(|_| \"super-secret\".repeat(3));\n   385→    let jwt_keys = JwtKeys::new(secret.as_bytes());\n   386→\n   387→    // Session key           .app_data(Data::new(jwt_keys.clone()))\n   398→            .wrap(Logger::default())\n   399→            .wrap(\n   400→                SessionMiddleware::builder(session_store.clone(), session_key.clone())\n   401→                    .session_lifecycle(PersistentSession::default().session_ttl(\n   402→                        Duration::from_secs(JWT_EXPIRY_SECS),\n   403→                    ))\n   404→                    .build(),\n   405→            )\n   406→            .service(create_pulse)\n   407→            .service(list_pulses)\n   408→    })\n   409→    .listen_openssl(\n   410→        listener,\n   411→        openssl::ssl::SslAcceptor::mozilla_modern(openssl::ssl::SslMethod::tls())?\n   412→            .set_private_key_file(tls_key, openssl::ssl::SslFiletype::PEM)?\n   413→            .set_certificate_chain_file(tls_cert)?,\n   414→    )?\n   415→    .run();\n   416→\n   417→    Ok(server)\n   418→}\n   419→\n   420→// ---------- Main ------------------------------------------------------------\n   421→\n   422→#[tokio::main]\n   423→async fn main() -> Result<()> {\n   424→    let db_url = std::env::var(\"EP_DATABASE_URL\").expect(\"PG URL\");\n   425→    let redis_url = std::env::var(\"EP_REDIS_URL\").unwrap_or_else(|_| \"redis://127.0.0.1/\".into());\n   426→    let bind_addr = std::env::var(\"EP_BIND_ADDR\").unwrap_or_else(|_| \"0.0.0.0:8443\".into());\n   427→\n   428→    let tls_key = std::env::var(\"EP_TLS_KEY\").unwrap_or_else(|_| \"certs/key.pem\".into());\n   429→ &tls_cert).await?;\n   432→\n   433→    // Graceful shutdown\n   434→    tokio::select! {\n   435→        res = server => res?,\n   436→        _ = signal::ctrl_c() => {\n   437→ API Quick Reference\n   480→\n   481→| Method | Path          | Description                         | Auth | Notes               |\n   482→|-------:|--------------:|-------------------------------------|------|---------------------|\n   483→| POST   | `/v1/pulses`  | Create a new Learning Pulse         | JWT  | Body: `title`, `description` |\n   484→| GET    | `/v1/pulses`  | List Learning Pulses by current user| JWT  | —                   |\n   485→\n   486→Request bodies are `application/json`; responses conform to the structures\n   487→illustrated in the example above.\n   488→\n   489→---\n   490→\n   491→## 3. Event Contract (`DomainEvent`)\n   492→\n   493→```jsonc\n   494→// Redis Stream entry\n   495→{\n   496→  \"event\": \"{                                      // JSON string\n   497→    \\\"PulseCreated\\\": {\n   498→      \\\"pulse_id\\\": \\\"bbd1-4a51-9d54-…\\\",\n   499→      \\\"teacher_id\\\": \\\"9f3d-a7c2-…\\\"\n   500→    }\n   501→  }\"\n   502→}\n   503→```\n   504→\n   505→**Down-stream services** (search indexer, mailer, analytics, etc.) subscribe\n   506→to the `edupulse_events` stream and handle their respective responsibilities,\n   507→keeping the HTTP API layer thin and responsive.\n   508→\n   509→---\n   510→\n   511→## 4. Security Checklist\n   512→✔ 100 % HTTPS with modern TLS (OpenSSL “Mozilla modern” profile)  \n   513→✔ Short-lived JWTs; sessions stored in Redis and automatically rotated  \n   514→✔ Column-level encryption is recommended for PII (out-of-scope here)  \n   515→✔ All secrets supplied via environment variables (12-factor compliant)  \n   516→\n   517→---\n   518→\n   519→Happy hacking! 🎓✨\n   520→```\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. End-to-End Example\nThe following single file demonstrates a _production-grade_, slice-through implementation\nshowing how EduPulse Live stitches together:\n\n* Service-layer + Repository pattern\n* Domain event publishing\n* JWT-based authentication middleware\n* Redis-backed session management\n* Search indexing (fire-and-forget)\n* TLS configuration\n\n```rust\n//! src/main.rs\n//! Run with: cargo run --features \"native-tls\"\n//!\n//! ATTENTION:  For brevity, error handling is simplified to `anyhow::Error`.\n//!             In production, design rich error types (crate `thiserror`).\n\nuse std::{net::TcpListener, sync::Arc, time::Duration};\n\nuse actix_session::{config::PersistentSession, storage::RedisSessionStore, SessionMiddleware};\nuse actix_web::{\n    cookie::Key,\n    dev::Server,\n    get, http::header, middleware::Logger, post,\n    web::{self, Data},\n    App, HttpResponse, HttpServer, tokio::{signal, task};\nuse uuid::Uuid;\n\n// ---------- Domain Model ----------------------------------------------------\n\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize, Clone)]\npub struct LearningPulse {\n    pub id: Uuid,\n    pub teacher_id: Uuid,\n    pub title: String,\n    pub description: String,\n    pub created_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n}\n\n// ---------- DTOs ------------------------------------------------------------\n\n#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]\npub struct CreatePulseRequest {\n    pub title: String,\n    pub description: // user id\n    exp: usize,\n}\n\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct JwtKeys {\n    enc: EncodingKey,\n    dec: DecodingKey,\n}\n\nimpl JwtKeys {\n    pub fn new(secret: &[u8]) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            enc: EncodingKey::from_secret(secret),\n            dec: DecodingKey::from_secret(secret),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\npub fn generate_jwt(user_id: Uuid, keys: &JwtKeys) -> Result<String> {\n    let claims = Claims {\n        sub: user_id.to_string(),\n        exp: (Utc::now().timestamp() as u64 + JWT_EXPIRY_SECS) as usize,\n    };\n    encode(&Header::default(), &claims, &keys.enc).context(\"sign jwt\")\n}\n\npub fn validate_jwt(token: &str, keys: &JwtKeys) -> Result<Uuid> {\n    let data = decode::<Claims>(\n        token,\n        &keys.dec,\n        &Validation::new(Algorithm::HS256),\n    )\n    .context(\"decode jwt\")?;\n    Ok(Uuid::parse_str(&data.claims.sub)?)\n}\n\n// ---------- Domain Events ---------------------------------------------------\n\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub enum DomainEvent {\n    PulseCreated { pulse_id: Uuid, teacher_id: Uuid },\n    // … more events (QuizSubmitted, BadgeGranted, etc.)\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventPublisher: Send + Sync {\n    async fn publish(&self, event: DomainEvent) -> Result<()>;\n}\n\npub struct RedisEventPublisher {\n    conn: ConnectionManager,\n}\n\nimpl RedisEventPublisher {\n    pub async fn new(redis_uri: &str) -> Result<Self> {\n        let client = redis::Client::open(redis_uri)?;\n        let conn = ConnectionManager::new(client).await?;\n        Ok(Self { conn })\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventPublisher for RedisEventPublisher {\n    async fn publish(&self, event: DomainEvent) -> Result<()> {\n        let payload = serde_json::to_string(&event)?;\n        let _: () = self\n            .conn\n            .clone()\n            .xadd(\"edupulse_events\", \"*\", &[(\"event\", &payload)])\n            .await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n// ---------- Repository Layer ------------------------------------------------\n\n#[async_trait]\npub trait PulseRepository: Send + Sync {\n    async fn create(&self, pulse: &LearningPulse) -> Result<()>;\n    async fn list_by_teacher(&self, teacher: Uuid) -> Result<Vec<LearningPulse>>;\n}\n\npub struct PgPulseRepository {\n    pool: PgPool,\n}\n\nimpl PgPulseRepository {\n    pub fn new(pool: PgPool) -> Self {\n        Self { pool }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl PulseRepository for PgPulseRepository {\n    async fn create(&self, pulse: &LearningPulse) -> Result<()> {\n        sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO learning_pulses (id, teacher_id, title, description, created_at)\n            VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5)\n            \"#,\n            pulse.id,\n            pulse.teacher_id,\n            pulse.title,\n            pulse.description,\n            pulse.created_at\n        )\n        .execute(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    async fn list_by_teacher(&self, teacher: Uuid) -> Result<Vec<LearningPulse>> {\n        let records = sqlx::query_as!(\n            LearningPulse,\n            r#\"\n            SELECT id, teacher_id, title, description, created_at\n            FROM learning_pulses\n            WHERE teacher_id = $1\n            ORDER BY created_at DESC\n            \"#,\n            teacher\n        )\n        .fetch_all(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n        Ok(records)\n    }\n}\n\n// ---------- Service Layer ---------------------------------------------------\n\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct PulseService {\n    repo: Arc<dyn PulseRepository>,\n    events: Arc<dyn EventPublisher>,\n    search: HttpClient,\n}\n\nimpl PulseService {\n    pub fn new(\n        repo: Arc<dyn PulseRepository>,\n        events: Arc<dyn EventPublisher>,\n        search: HttpClient,\n    ) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            repo,\n            events,\n            search,\n        }\n    }\n\n    pub async fn create_pulse(\n        &self,\n        teacher_id: Uuid,\n        req: CreatePulseRequest,\n    ) -> Result<LearningPulse> {\n        let pulse = LearningPulse {\n            id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            teacher_id,\n            title: req.title,\n            description: req.description,\n            created_at: Utc::now(),\n        };\n\n        self.repo.create(&pulse).await?;\n\n        // Fire-and-forget search indexing\n        let search_clone = self.search.clone();\n        let .json(&pulse_clone)\n                .send()\n                .await;\n        });\n\n        // Publish domain event\n        self.events\n            .publish(DomainEvent::PulseCreated {\n  ///\n   255→    /// This method is *synchronous* with respect to the caller’s DB\n   256→    /// transaction; the actual dispatch happens in the background\n   257→    /// daemon spawned by `run_dispatch_loop`.\n   258→    pub async fn stage_event<E: DomainEvent>(\n   259→        &self,\n   260→        tx: &mut Transaction<'_, Postgres>,\n   261→        aggregate_id: Uuid,\n   262→        event: &E,\n   263→    ) -> Result<(), OutboxError> {\n   264→        let payload = serde_json::to_value(event)\n   265→            .map_err(|e| OutboxError::Other(format!(\"serde: {e}\")))?;\n   266→\n   267→        let mut unsigned = EventEnvelope {\n   268→            id:           Uuid::new_v4(),\n   269→            aggregate_id,\n   270→            event_type: unsent events and publishes them.\n   282→    ///\n   283→    /// Should be run in its own Tokio task; gracefully stops when the\n   284→    /// task is cancelled.\n   285→    pub async fn run_dispatch_loop(self: Arc<Self>) -> Result<(), OutboxError> {\n   286→        let nats_conn = async_nats::connect(self.nats_url.clone()).await?;\n   287→\n   288→        loop {\n   289→            // Fetch up to `max_batch` events that still need sending.\n   290→            let events = self.repo.fetch_unsent(self.max_batch).await?;\n   291→\n   292→            if events.is_empty() {\n   293→                sleep(self.poll_every).await;\n   294→                continue;\n   295→            }\n   296→\n   297→            // We use a `FuturesUnordered` stream to publish in parallel\n   298→            // while still limiting overall memory / network utilisation.\n   299→            let publish_fut = stream::iter(events.clone())\n   300→                .map(|evt| {\n   301→                    let conn = &nats_conn;\n   302→                    async move {\n   303→                        conn.publish(evt.subject(), serde_json::to_vec(&evt)?)\n   304→                            .await\n   305→                            .map_err(OutboxError::from)\n   306→                    }\n   307→                })\n   308→                .buffer_unordered(16)\n   309→                .collect::<Vec<_>>();\n   310→\n   311→            let results = publish_fut.await;\n   312→\n   313→            // Separate errors from successes for partial retries.\n   314→            let (oks, errs): (Vec<_>, Vec<_>) =\n   315→                results.into_iter().partition(Result::is_ok);\n   316→\n   317→            if !oks.is_empty() {\n   318→                // Only mark events sent when the publish actually succeeded.\n   319→                let sent_events: Vec<_> = events\n   320→                    .iter()\n   321→                    .zip(oks)\n   322→                    .filter_map(|(evt, res)| res.ok().map(|_| evt.clone()))\n   323→                    .collect();\n   324→\n   325→                self.repo.mark_sent(&sent_events).await?;\n   326→            }\n   327→\n   328→            if !errs.is_empty() {\n   329→                // Log and backoff; caller decides global error handling.\n   330→                tracing::warn!(\n   331→                    failed = errs.len(),\n   332→                    \"Some messages failed to publish; will retry\"\n   333→                );\n   334→                // Exponential-ish backoff for noisy neighbours.\n   335→                sleep(self.poll_every * 2).await;\n   336→            }\n   337→        }\n   338→    }\n   339→\n   340→    fn sign_payload(&self, envelope: &EventEnvelope) -> Result<String, OutboxError> {\n   341→        let mut bytes = serde_json::to_vec(envelope)\n   342→            .map_err(|e| OutboxError::Other(format!(\"serde: {e}\")))?;\n   343→\n   344→        // Sign the raw bytes (comment this if you prefer canonical JSON).\n   345→        let sig = self.key_pair.sign(&mut bytes);\n   346→        Ok(base64::encode(sig.as_ref()))\n   347→    }\n   348→}\n   349→\n   350→/// Example domain event.\n   351→///\n   352→/// In real code these live inside domain modules; kept here for demo.\n   353→#[derive(Debug, Serialize)]\n   354→pub struct LessonPublished {\n Outbox/Event‐Sourcing infrastructure for EduPulse Live.\n//!\n//! This module implements the Outbox pattern and provides\n//! secure, reliable publishing of domain events to the system\n//! message broker (NATS in the reference deployment).\n//!\n//! The component is independent from the HTTP layer; it can be\n//! embedded in the main API service or scheduled as a separate,\n//! dedicated binary.  All I/O is asynchronous and cancellation-\n//! safe.  Database access is handled through `sqlx` with compile-\n//! time query checking.  Messages are signed with an Ed25519 key\n//! pair to guarantee authenticity and integrity before being\n//! shipped to external subscribers.\n//!\n//! Dependencies (Cargo.toml excerpt):\n//! ----------------------------------\n//! [dependencies]\n//! async-trait = \"0.1\"\n//! chrono       = { version = \"0.4\", features = [\"serde\"] }\n//! futures      = \"0.3\"\n//! nats         = { version = \"0.23\", default-features = false, features = [\"tls\"] }\n//! ring         = \"0.16\"\n//! serde        = { version = \"1.0\", features = [\"derive\"] }\n//! serde_json   = \"1.0\"\n//! sqlx         = { version = \"0.7\", features = [\"runtime-tokio-rustls\", \"postgres\", \"chrono\", \"uuid\", \"json\"] }\n//! tokio        = { version = \"1\", features = [\"macros\", \"rt-multi-thread\", \"time\"] }\n//! uuid         = { version = \"1\", features = [\"serde\", \"v4\"] }\n//!\n//! # Optional (for local CLI tooling / migrations)\n//! sqlx-cli     = { version = \"0.7\", default-features = false, features = [\"postgres\"] }\n//!\n//! Database schema (PostgreSQL):\n//! -----------------------------\n//! ```sql\n//! CREATE TABLE event_outbox (\n//!     id              UUID            PRIMARY KEY,\n//!     aggregate_id    UUID            NOT NULL,\n//!     event_type      TEXT            NOT NULL,\n//!     payload         JSONB           NOT NULL,\n//!     occurred_at     TIMESTAMPTZ     NOT NULL,\n//!     signature       TEXT            NOT NULL,\n//!     sent_at         TIMESTAMPTZ\n//! );\n//! CREATE INDEX ON event_outbox (sent_at) WHERE sent_at IS NULL;\n//! ```\n//!\n//! ------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nuse std::sync::Arc;\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\nuse futures::{stream, StreamExt, TryFutureExt};\nuse ring::signature::{Ed25519KeyPair, KeyPair};\nuse trait for serializable, JSON–compatible\n/// domain events originating from the core business logic.\npub trait DomainEvent: Serialize + Send + Sync + 'static {\n    /// Canonical name of the event (e.g., \"lesson.published.v1\").\n    fn event_type(&self) -> &'static str;\n}\n\n/// Envelope stored in the outbox table.\n///\n/// The struct doubles as the wire format that gets shipped through\n/// the message broker (subject: `edupulse.events.<event_type>`).\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct EventEnvelope {\n    pub id:           Uuid,\n    pub aggregate_id: Uuid,\n    pub event_type:   String,\n    pub payload:      serde_json::Value,\n    pub occurred_at:  DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub signature:    String,\n}\n\nimpl EventEnvelope {\n    /// Returns the NATS subject for this event.\n    pub fn subject(&self) -> String {\n        format!(\"edupulse.events.{}\", self.event_type)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Custom error type bubbles up all possible failure modes.\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum OutboxError {\n    #[error(\"database error: {0}\")]\n    Db(#[from] sqlx::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"nats I/O error: {0}\")]\n    Nats(#[from] async_nats::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"crypto error\")]\n    Crypto,\n\n    #[error(\"unexpected: {0}\")]\n    Other(String),\n}\n\n/// Repository trait hides persistence details (useful for tests).\n#[async_trait]\npub trait OutboxRepository {\n    async fn insert(\n        &self,\n        tx: &mut Transaction<'_, Postgres>,\n        envelope: &EventEnvelope,\n    ) -> Result<(), OutboxError>;\n\n    async fn fetch_unsent(\n        &self,\n        limit: i64,\n    ) -> Result<Vec<EventEnvelope>, OutboxError>;\n\n    async fn mark_sent(\n        &self,\n        envelopes: &[EventEnvelope],\n    ) -> Result<(), OutboxError>;\n}\n\n/// Concrete PostgreSQL implementation.\npub struct PgOutboxRepository {\n    pool: PgPool,\n}\n\nimpl PgOutboxRepository {\n    pub fn new(pool: PgPool) -> Self {\n        Self { pool }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl OutboxRepository for PgOutboxRepository {\n    async fn insert(\n        &self,\n        tx: &mut Transaction<'_, Postgres>,\n        envelope: &EventEnvelope,\n    ) -> Result<(), OutboxError> {\n        sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO event_outbox (\n                id,\n                aggregate_id,\n                event_type,\n                payload,\n                occurred_at,\n                signature\n            )\n            VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5, $6)\n            \"#,\n            envelope.id,\n            envelope.aggregate_id,\n            envelope.event_type,\n            envelope.payload,\n            envelope.occurred_at,\n            envelope.signature\n        )\n        .execute(&mut **tx)\n        .await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    async fn fetch_unsent(\n        &self,\n        limit: i64,\n    ) -> Result<Vec<EventEnvelope>, OutboxError> {\n        let rows = sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT id,\n                   aggregate_id,\n                   event_type,\n                   payload,\n                   occurred_at,\n                   signature\n            FROM event_outbox\n            WHERE sent_at IS NULL\n            ORDER BY occurred_at\n            LIMIT $1\n            \"#,\n            limit\n        )\n        .fetch_all(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        Ok(rows\n            .into_iter()\n            .map(|r| EventEnvelope {\n                id: r.id,\n                aggregate_id: r.aggregate_id,\n                event_type: r.event_type,\n                payload: r.payload,\n                occurred_at: r.occurred_at,\n                signature: r.signature,\n            })\n            .collect())\n    }\n\n    async fn mark_sent(\n        &self,\n        envelopes: &[EventEnvelope],\n    ) -> Result<(), OutboxError> {\n        if envelopes.is_empty() {\n            return Ok(());\n        }\n        let ids: Vec<Uuid> = envelopes.iter().map(|e| e.id).collect();\n        sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            UPDATE event_outbox\n            SET sent_at = NOW()\n            WHERE id = ANY($1)\n            \"#,\n            &ids[..]\n        )\n        .execute(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/// Outbox service coordinates persistence and dispatching.\npub struct OutboxService<R: OutboxRepository + Send + Sync> {\n    repo:        Arc<R>,\n    nats_url:    String,\n    key_pair:    Arc<Ed25519KeyPair>,\n    // Retry/backoff configuration.\n    max_batch:   i64,\n    poll_every:  Duration,\n}\n\nimpl<R: OutboxRepository + Send + Sync> OutboxService<R> {\n    pub fn new(\n        repo: Arc<R>,\n        nats_url: impl Into<String>,\n        key_pair: Ed25519KeyPair,\n    ) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            repo,\n            nats_url: nats_url.into(),\n            key_pair: Arc::new(key_pair),\n            max_batch: 256,\n            poll_every: Duration::from_secs(2),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Persists a domain event within an existing transaction.\n    ///\n    /// This method is *synchronous* with respect to the caller’s DB\n    /// transaction; the actual dispatch happens in the background\n    /// daemon spawned by `run_dispatch_loop`.\n    pub async fn stage_event<E: DomainEvent>(\n        &self,\n        tx: &mut Transaction<'_, Postgres>,\n        aggregate_id: Uuid,\n        event: &E,\n    ) -> Result<(), OutboxError> {\n        let payload = serde_json::to_value(event)\n            .map_err(|e| OutboxError::Other(format!(\"serde: {e}\")))?;\n\n        let mut unsigned = EventEnvelope {\n            id:           Uuid::new_v4(),\n            aggregate_id,\n            event_type:   event.event_type().to_owned(),\n            payload,\n            occurred_at:  Utc::now(),\n            signature:    String::new(), // placeholder\n        };\n\n        unsigned.signature = self.sign_payload(&unsigned)?;\n\n        self.repo.insert(tx, &unsigned).await\n    }\n\n    /// Infinite loop: regularly pulls unsent events and publishes them.\n    ///\n    /// Should be run in its own Tokio task; gracefully stops when the\n    /// task is cancelled.\n    pub async fn run_dispatch_loop(self: Arc<Self>) -> Result<(), OutboxError> {\n        let nats_conn = async_nats::connect(self.nats_url.clone()).await?;\n\n        loop {\n            // Fetch up to `max_batch` events that still need sending.\n            let events = self.repo.fetch_unsent(self.max_batch).await?;\n\n            if events.is_empty() {\n                sleep(self.poll_every).await;\n                continue;\n            }\n\n            // We use a `FuturesUnordered` stream to publish in parallel\n            // while still limiting overall memory / network utilisation.\n            let publish_fut = stream::iter(events.clone())\n                .map(|evt| {\n                    let conn = &nats_conn;\n                    async move {\n                        conn.publish(evt.subject(), serde_json::to_vec(&evt)?)\n                            .await\n                            .map_err(OutboxError::from)\n                    }\n                })\n                .buffer_unordered(16)\n                .collect::<Vec<_>>();\n\n            let results = publish_fut.await;\n\n            // Separate errors from successes for partial retries.\n            let (oks, errs): (Vec<_>, Vec<_>) =\n                results.into_iter().partition(Result::is_ok);\n\n            if !oks.is_empty() {\n                // Only mark events sent when the publish actually succeeded.\n                let sent_events: Vec<_> = events\n                    .iter()\n                    .zip(oks)\n                    .filter_map(|(evt, res)| res.ok().map(|_| evt.clone()))\n                    .collect();\n\n                self.repo.mark_sent(&sent_events).await?;\n            }\n\n            if !errs.is_empty() {\n                // Log and backoff; caller decides global error handling.\n                tracing::warn!(\n                    failed = errs.len(),\n                    \"Some messages failed to publish; will retry\"\n                );\n                // Exponential-ish backoff for noisy neighbours.\n                sleep(self.poll_every * 2).await;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn sign_payload(&self, envelope: &EventEnvelope) -> Result<String, OutboxError> {\n        let mut bytes = serde_json::to_vec(envelope)\n            .map_err(|e| OutboxError::Other(format!(\"serde: {e}\")))?;\n\n        // Sign the raw bytes (comment this if you prefer canonical JSON).\n        let sig = self.key_pair.sign(&mut bytes);\n        Ok(base64::encode(sig.as_ref()))\n    }\n}\n\n/// Example domain event.\n///\n/// In real code these live inside domain modules; kept here for demo.\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize)]\npub struct LessonPublished {\n    pub lesson_id: Uuid,\n    pub title:     String,\n    pub author_id: Uuid,\n    pub tags:      Vec<String>,\n}\n\nimpl DomainEvent for LessonPublished {\n    fn event_type(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"lesson.published.v1\"\n    }\n}\n\n/// Convenience helper for crafting an Ed25519 key pair from disk/env.\n///\n/// In production you would keep the private key in Hashicorp Vault\n/// or a TPM; this simplistic helper is for local development only.\npub fn load_signing_key() -> Result<Ed25519KeyPair, OutboxError> {\n    use ring::rand::SystemRandom;\n    use ring::signature::Ed25519KeyPair as Key;\n\n    if let Ok(pkcs8_b64) = std::env::var(\"EDUPULSE_SIGNING_KEY_B64\") {\n        let bytes = base64::decode(pkcs8_b64)\n            .map_err(|_| OutboxError::Crypto)?;\n        Key::from_pkcs8(&bytes).map_err(|_| OutboxError::Crypto)\n    } else {\n        // Auto-generate volatile key when none provided.\n        let rng = SystemRandom::new();\n        let pkcs8 = Key::generate_pkcs8(&rng).map_err(|_| OutboxError::Crypto)?;\n        Ok(Key::from_pkcs8(pkcs8.as_ref()).map_err(|_| OutboxError::Crypto)?)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Bootstraps a producer demo.\n///\n/// This async function illustrates how to embed the Outbox transaction\n/// into domain service logic.  Remove or adjust for production usage.\npub async fn demo_produce_event(db_url: &str) -> Result<(), OutboxError> {\n    let pool = PgPoolOptions::new()\n        .max_connections(5)\n        .connect(db_url)\n        .await?;\n\n    let repo = Arc::new(PgOutboxRepository::new(pool.clone()));\n    let key_pair = load_signing_key()?;\n    let outbox = Arc::new(OutboxService::new(\n        repo.clone(),\n        \"tls://connect.ngs.global\".to_string(),\n        key_pair,\n    ));\n\n    // spawn the background dispatcher\n    tokio::spawn(outbox.clone().run_dispatch_loop());\n\n    // Business transaction: create lesson, persist event, commit.\n    let mut tx = pool.begin().await?;\n\n    // Imagine we create a lesson in lessons table here …\n\n    let event = LessonPublished {\n        lesson_id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n        title:     \"Algebraic Topology 101\".to_owned(),\n        author_id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n        tags:      vec![\"math\".into(), \"topology\".into()],\n    };\n\n    outbox\n        .stage_event(&mut tx, event.lesson_id, &event)\n        .await?;\n\n    tx.commit().await?;\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n```","numLines":428,"startLine":1,"totalLines":428}}}

Learning Pulse domain module.\n//!\n//! This module owns the core workflow for creating and replying to\n//! “learning pulses”.  It follows the Service-Layer + Repository pattern,\n//! emits domain events, and is fully asynchronous.\n//!\n//! The implementation intentionally avoids leaking concrete I/O details\n//! to ease unit testing—external concerns (SQL database, message broker)\n//! are injected behind traits.\n//!\n//! Dependencies (add to Cargo.toml if missing):\n//!   async-trait    = \"0.1\"\n//!   chrono         = { version = \"0.4\", features = [\"serde\"] }\n//!   serde          = { version = \"1\", features = [\"derive\"] }\n//!   sqlx           = { version = \"0.7\", default-features = false, features = [\"rustls\",\"postgres\",\"macros\",\"chrono\"] }\n//!   thiserror      = \"1\"\n//!\n//! The rest of the crate is expected to provide real implementations of\n//! `EventPublisher` and `PgPool`.\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse sqlx::{postgres::PgRow, PgPool, Row};\nuse thiserror::Error;\nuse uuid::Uuid;\n\n/// Domain object representing a learning pulse (challenge/activity).\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct LearningPulse {\n    pub id: Uuid,\n    pub author_id: Uuid,\n    pub title: String,\n    pub body: String,\n    pub created_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub due_at: Uuid,\n    pub pulse_id: Uuid,\n    pub responder_id: Uuid,\n    pub content_uri: String,\n    pub submitted_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n}\n\n/// Events the module can publish downstream.\n///\n/// NOTE: Versioned event naming scheme, e.g. `PulseCreated.v1`,\n/// guarantees forward compatibility when the payload shape changes.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(tag = \"type\", content = \"data\")]\npub enum PulseEvent {\n    #[serde(rename = \"PulseCreated.v1\")]\n    PulseCreated {\n        pulse_id: Uuid,\n        author_id: Uuid,\n        title: String,\n    },\n    #[serde(rename = \"PulseResponded.v1\")]\n    PulseResponded {\n        pulse_id: Uuid,\n        responder_id: Uuid,\n        response_id: Uuid,\n        content_uri: String,\n    },\n}\n\n/// Injectable message publisher for decoupling side-effects.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventPublisher: Send + Sync + 'static {\n    async fn publish(&self, event: PulseEvent) -> Result<(), PublishError>;\n}\n\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum PublishError {\n    #[error(\"broker disconnected\")]\n    Disconnected,\n    #[error(\"serialization error: {0}\")]\n    Serialization(#[from] serde_json::Error),\n    #[error(\"unknown publish error: {0}\")]\n    Other(String),\n}\n\n/// Persistence layer errors.\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum RepositoryError {\n    #[error(\"entity not found\")]\n    NotFound,\n    #[error(\"database error: {0}\")]\n    Db(#[from] sqlx::Error),\n}\n\n/// Service-level error enumeration.\n///\n/// Explicit conversion From<RepositoryError> is implemented so\n/// caller code can use `?` without additional noise.\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum PulseServiceError {\n    #[error(\"input validation failed: {0}\")]\n    Validation(String),\n    #[error(transparent)]\n    Repository(#[from] RepositoryError),\n    #[error(transparent)]\n    Publisher(#[from] PublishError),\n}\n\n/// Repository abstraction for learning pulses.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait PulseRepository: Send + Sync + 'static {\n    async fn insert(&self, pulse: &LearningPulse) -> Result<(), RepositoryError>;\n    async fn find(&self, id: Uuid) -> Result<LearningPulse, RepositoryError>;\n    async fn archive(&self, id: Uuid) -> Result<(), RepositoryError>;\n}\n\n/// Repository abstraction for pulse responses.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait ResponseRepository: Send + Sync + 'static {\n    async fn insert(&self, resp: &PulseResponse) -> Result<(), RepositoryError>;\n    async fn list_for_pulse(&self, pulse_id: Uuid) -> Result<Vec<PulseResponse>, RepositoryError>;\n}\n\n/// Concrete Postgres implementation of `PulseRepository`.\npub struct PgPulseRepository {\n    pool: PgPool,\n}\n\nimpl PgPulseRepository {\n    pub fn new(pool: PgPool) -> Self {\n        Self { pool }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl PulseRepository for PgPulseRepository {\n    async fn insert(&self, pulse: &LearningPulse) -> Result<(), RepositoryError> {\n        sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO learning_pulses (id, author_id, title, body, created_at, due_at, is_archived)\n            VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5, $6, $7)\n            \"#,\n            pulse.id,\n            pulse.author_id,\n            pulse.title,\n            pulse.body,\n            pulse.created_at,\n            pulse.due_at,\n            pulse.is_archived\n        )\n        .execute(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    async fn find(&self, id: Uuid) -> Result<LearningPulse, RepositoryError> {\n        let row: PgRow = sqlx::query(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT id, author_id, title, body, created_at, due_at, is_archived\n            FROM learning_pulses\n            WHERE id = $1\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(id)\n        .fetch_one(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        Ok(LearningPulse {\n            id: row.get(\"id\"),\n            author_id: row.get(\"author_id\"),\n            title: row.get(\"title\"),\n            body: row.get(\"body\"),\n            created_at: row.get(\"created_at\"),\n            due_at: row.try_get(\"due_at\")?,\n            is_archived: row.get(\"is_archived\"),\n        })\n    }\n\n    async fn archive(&self, id: Uuid) -> Result<(), RepositoryError> {\n        let res = sqlx::query!(\n            \"UPDATE learning_pulses SET is_archived = TRUE WHERE id = $1\",\n            id\n        )\n        .execute(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        if res.rows_affected() == 0 {\n            Err(RepositoryError::NotFound)\n        } else {\n            Ok(())\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Concrete Postgres implementation of `ResponseRepository`.\npub struct PgResponseRepository {\n    pool: PgPool,\n}\n\nimpl PgResponseRepository {\n    pub fn new(pool: PgPool) -> Self {\n        Self { pool }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl ResponseRepository for PgResponseRepository {\n    async fn insert(&self, resp: VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5)\n            \"#,\n            resp.id,\n            resp.pulse_id,\n            resp.responder_id,\n            resp.content_uri,\n            resp.submitted_at\n        )\n        .execute(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    async fn list_for_pulse(&self, pulse_id: Uuid) -> Result<Vec<PulseResponse>, RepositoryError> {\n        let rows = sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT id, pulse_id, responder_id, content_uri, submitted_at\n            FROM pulse_responses\n            WHERE pulse_id = $1\n            \"#,\n            pulse_id\n        )\n        .fetch_all(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        Ok(rows\n            .into_iter()\n            .map(|r| PulseResponse {\n                id: r.id,\n                pulse_id: r.pulse_id,\n                responder_id: r.responder_id,\n                content_uri: r.content_uri,\n                submitted_at: r.submitted_at,\n            })\n            .collect())\n    }\n}\n\n/// Service facade—entry point used by controllers / GraphQL resolvers.\npub struct PulseService<R: PulseRepository, S: ResponseRepository, P: EventPublisher> {\n    pulses: {\n        Self {\n            pulses,\n            responses,\n            publisher,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Creates a new learning pulse and publishes `PulseCreated` event.\n    pub async fn create_pulse(\n        &self,\n        author_id: Uuid,\n        title: &str,\n        body: &str,\n        due_at: Option<DateTime<Utc>>,\n    ) -> Result<LearningPulse, PulseServiceError> {\n        if title.trim().is_empty() {\n            return Err(PulseServiceError::Validation(\n                \"title must not be empty\".into(),\n            ));\n        }\n\n        let pulse = LearningPulse {\n            id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            author_id,\n            title: title.trim().to_owned(),\n            body: body.to_owned(),\n            created_at: Utc::now(),\n            due_at,\n            is_archived: false,\n        };\n\n        self.pulses.insert(&pulse).await?;\n\n        // Fire-and-forget the event; let caller decide whether to await or spawn.\n        self.publisher\n            .publish(PulseEvent::PulseCreated {\n                pulse_id: pulse.id,\n                author_id: pulse.author_id,\n                title: pulse.title.clone(),\n            })\n            .await?;\n\n        Ok(pulse)\n    }\n\n    /// Adds a student response to a pulse and publishes `PulseResponded`.\n    pub async fn respond_to_pulse(\n        &self,\n        pulse_id: Uuid,\n        responder_id: Uuid,\n        content_uri: &str,\n    ) -> Result<PulseResponse, PulseServiceError> {\n        // Validate pulse existence.\n        let pulse = self.pulses.find(pulse_id).await?;\n        if pulse.is_archived {\n            return Err(PulseServiceError::Validation(\n                \"Cannot respond to an archived pulse\".into(),\n            ));\n        }\n\n        if content_uri.trim().is_empty() {\n            return Err(PulseServiceError::Validation(\n                \"content_uri must not be empty\".into(),\n            ));\n        }\n\n        let resp = PulseResponse {\n            id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            pulse_id,\n            responder_id,\n            content_uri: content_uri.trim().to_owned(),\n            submitted_at: Utc::now(),\n        };\n\n        self.responses.insert(&resp).await?;\n\n        self.publisher\n            .publish(PulseEvent::PulseResponded {\n                pulse_id,\n                responder_id,\n                response_id: resp.id,\n                content_uri: resp.content_uri.clone(),\n            })\n            .await?;\n\n        Ok(resp)\n    }\n\n    /// Returns all responses for a given pulse.\n    pub async fn list_responses(\n        &self,\n        pulse_id: Uuid,\n    ) -> Result<Vec<PulseResponse>, PulseServiceError> {\n        Ok(self.responses.list_for_pulse(pulse_id).await?)\n    }\n\n    /// Archives a learning pulse (soft-delete).\n    pub async fn archive_pulse(&self, pulse_id: Uuid) -> Result<(), PulseServiceError> {\n        self.pulses.archive(pulse_id).await?;\n        // Future: publish `PulseArchived` event here\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/* ------------------------------------------------------------\n .create_pulse(author, \"Rust Ownership Quiz\", \"Explain ownership.\", None)\n            .await?;\n\n        assert_eq!(pulse.title, \"Rust Ownership Quiz\");\n\n        let response = service\n }\n}\n```","numLines":466,"startLine":1,"totalLines":466}}}

Core Architectural Components Identified\n\n| Component | Module(s) | Technology | Role |\n|-----------|-----------|------------|------|\n| **API Gateway** | module_1, module_49, docs/api.md | Actix-Web / Axum | HTTP/HTTPS endpoint routing, request validation, middleware |\n| **Authentication** | module_1, module_49 | JWT (jsonwebtoken), Argon2, Redis | User registration, login, token validation, session management |\n| **Event Publishing** | module_2, module_4, module_5, module_7 | RabbitMQ (lapin), NATS (async-nats) | Domain event emission to message broker |\n| **Write Model** | module_3, module_10, module_22, module_65 | PostgreSQL (sqlx/diesel) | Command handling, aggregate persistence |\n| **Event Consumers** | module_15, module_68, module_76 | Tokio broadcast, Message broker subscriptions | React to events, update read models, trigger side effects |\n| **Outbox Processor** | module_9, module_11, module_50, module_77 | PostgreSQL + Polling | Reliable at-least-once event delivery |\n\n### 2. Architectural Patterns Identified\n\n**Event-Driven Architecture (EDA)**\n- All state changes emit strongly-typed domain events\n- Events are published to RabbitMQ/NATS for downstream processing\n- Loose coupling between bounded contexts\n\n**CQRS (Command Query Responsibility Segregation)**\n- Evidence: Separate configuration for write database and read-optimized stores\n- Commands (create pulse, submit response) handled by service layer\n- Queries served from denormalized read models (Elasticsearch/MeiliSearch for search)\n\n**Transactional Outbox Pattern**\n- Events persisted in `event_outbox` table within the same database transaction\n- Background `OutboxProcessor` polls for unpublished events and dispatches to broker\n- Guarantees at-least-once delivery without distributed transactions\n\n**Repository Pattern**\n- Abstract `PulseRepository`, `ResponseRepository` traits\n- Concrete PostgreSQL implementations (PgPulseRepository, PgResponseRepository)\n- Enables easy testing with in-memory implementations\n\n**Service Layer Pattern**\n- Business logic encapsulated in services (AuthService, LearningPulseService, BadgeAwardService)\n- Services coordinate repositories, validation, and event emission\n\n### 3. Data Flow: Creating a New Post (Learning Pulse) in a Study Group\n\nSince the codebase uses \"Learning Pulse\" as the primary content entity (similar to a \"Post\" in study groups), I'll trace this flow:\n\n**Step 1: API Request → API Gateway (module_49)**\n```\nClient → POST /pulse/response (or /v1/pulses)\n       → Actix-Web Router extracts JWT from Authorization header\n       → AuthenticatedUser extractor validates token\n```\n\n**Step 2: Authentication Check (module_1)**\n```\nJWT token → DecodingKey validation → Redis session lookup → User ID extraction\n```\n\n**Step 3: Service Layer Processing (module_22)**\n```\nPulseService.create_pulse(author_id, title, body, due_at)\n  → Validation (empty title check)\n  → Create LearningPulse entity with Uuid::new_v4()\n  → PulseRepository.insert(&pulse) → PostgreSQL INSERT\n```\n\n**Step 4: Event Emission (module_4 / module_11)**\n```\nEventPublisher.publish(PulseEvent::PulseCreated {...})\n  → Serialize to JSON\n  → Insert into event_outbox table (same transaction) OR\n  → Publish directly to RabbitMQ/NATS\n```\n\n**Step 5: Outbox Processor (module_11 / module_50)**\n```\nOutboxProcessor.tick()\n  → SELECT * FROM event_outbox WHERE published_at IS NULL\n  → For each event: nats.publish(subject, payload)\n  → UPDATE event_outbox SET published_at = NOW()\n```\n\n**Step 6: Event Consumers (module_15 / module_76)**\n```\nSearchIndexer (module_76)\n  → Subscribe to \"edupulse.web_social.learning_pulse_created\"\n  → Index document in Elasticsearch/MeiliSearch\n\nBadgeAwardService (module_15)\n  → Subscribe to relevant events\n  → Check badge eligibility\n  → Award badges if criteria met\n```\n\n### 4. Proposed Integration Strategy: Real-time Collaborative Whiteboard\n\n#### New Service Architecture\n\n**WhiteboardService** - New WebSocket-based microservice for real-time drawing collaboration\n\n**Key Design Decisions:**\n\n1. **WebSocket Communication (Not Event Bus)**\n   - Drawing actions require sub-50ms latency\n   - RabbitMQ/NATS adds 10-100ms latency per message hop\n   - Use dedicated WebSocket connections with room-based broadcasting\n\n2. **State Management**\n   - **Hot State**: Redis with Sorted Sets for operation ordering (CRDT-friendly)\n   - **Cold State**: PostgreSQL for persistent whiteboard snapshots\n   - **Snapshotting**: Every N operations or M seconds, persist full state\n\n3. **Authentication Integration**\n   - Reuse existing JWT validation from module_1\n   - WebSocket upgrade requires valid session token\n   - Room access verified against study group membership\n\n4. **Event Bus Integration**\n   - Emit `WhiteboardSessionStarted`, `WhiteboardSessionEnded` to main event bus\n   - Enable downstream analytics, notifications\n   - Main platform stays eventually consistent with whiteboard state\n\n#### Proposed Components\n\n| Component | Technology | Purpose |\n|-----------|------------|---------|\n| **WebSocket Server** | tokio-tungstenite / axum-websocket | Real-time bidirectional communication |\n| **Room Manager** | In-memory HashMap + Redis Pub/Sub | Track connected clients per whiteboard room |\n| **Operation Buffer** | Redis Streams | Order and buffer drawing operations |\n| **Snapshot Store** | PostgreSQL JSONB | Persist whiteboard state periodically |\n| **Auth Middleware** | JWT validation (shared with module_1) | Validate user identity on connect |\n\n#### Data Flow for Drawing Action\n\n```\n1. Client draws → WebSocket message to WhiteboardService\n2. WhiteboardService validates session, authorizes room access\n3. Operation appended to Redis Stream (whiteboard:{room_id}:ops)\n4. Broadcast operation to all clients in room via WebSocket\n5. Every 30 seconds: Snapshot full canvas state to PostgreSQL\n6. On session end: Emit WhiteboardSessionEnded to RabbitMQ\n```\n\n## MermaidJS Diagrams\n\n### Diagram 1: Existing Architecture (Create Pulse Flow)\n\n```mermaid\ngraph TD\n    subgraph Client\n        A[Web/Mobile Client]\n    end\n\n    subgraph API_Gateway[\"API Gateway (module_49)\"]\n        B[Actix-Web Router]\n        C[JWT Middleware]\n    end\n\n    subgraph Auth[\"Authentication (module_1)\"]\n        D[AuthService]\n        E[Redis Session Store]\n    end\n\n    subgraph Service_Layer[\"Service Layer (module_22)\"]\n        F[PulseService]\n        G[Validation]\n    end\n\n    subgraph Write_Model[\"Write Model\"]\n        H[(PostgreSQL)]\n        I[PulseRepository]\n    end\n\n    subgraph Outbox[\"Outbox (module_11)\"]\n        J[event_outbox table]\n        K[OutboxProcessor]\n    end\n\n    subgraph Message_Broker[\"Message Broker\"]\n        L[RabbitMQ / NATS]\n    end\n\n    subgraph Consumers[\"Event Consumers\"]\n        M[SearchIndexer - module_76]\n        N[BadgeAwardService - module_15]\n        O[AnalyticsWorker]\n    end\n\n    subgraph Read_Model[\"Read Models\"]\n        P[(Elasticsearch)]\n        Q[(Redis Cache)]\n    end\n\n    A -->|POST /v1/pulses| B\n    B --> C\n    C -->|Validate JWT| D\n    D --> E\n    C -->|Authorized| F\n    F --> G\n    G -->|Valid| I\n    I -->|INSERT| H\n    I -->|INSERT| J\n    J --> K\n    K -->|Publish| L\n    L --> M\n    L --> N\n    L --> O\n    M -->|Index| P\n    N -->|Query/Update| H\n```\n\n### Diagram 2: Proposed Architecture with Collaborative Whiteboard\n\n```mermaid\ngraph TD\n    subgraph Client\n        A1[Web Client]\n        A2[Drawing Canvas]\n    end\n\n    subgraph API_Gateway[\"API Gateway (module_49)\"]\n        B[HTTP Router]\n        C[JWT Middleware]\n    end\n\n    subgraph Whiteboard_Service[\"NEW: Whiteboard Service\"]\n        WS1[WebSocket Server]\n        WS2[Room Manager]\n        WS3[Operation Validator]\n        WS4[Broadcast Engine]\n    end\n\n    subgraph Hot_State[\"Hot State\"]\n        HS1[(Redis Pub/Sub)]\n        HS2[(Redis Streams)]\n    end\n\n    subgraph Cold_State[\"Cold State\"]\n        CS1[(PostgreSQL - Snapshots)]\n    end\n\n    subgraph Auth[\"Authentication (module_1)\"]\n        D[AuthService]\n        E[Redis Session Store]\n    end\n\n    subgraph Service_Layer[\"Service Layer (module_22)\"]\n        F[PulseService]\n    end\n\n    subgraph Write_Model[\"Write Model\"]\n        H[(PostgreSQL - Main)]\n        I[PulseRepository]\n    end\n\n    subgraph Outbox[\"Outbox (module_11)\"]\n        J[event_outbox]\n        K[OutboxProcessor]\n    end\n\n    subgraph Message_Broker[\"Message Broker\"]\n        L[RabbitMQ / NATS]\n    end\n\n    subgraph Consumers[\"Event Consumers\"]\n        M[SearchIndexer]\n        N[BadgeAwardService]\n        P[NotificationService]\n    end\n\n    A1 -->|HTTP REST| B\n    B --> C\n    C --> D\n    D --> E\n\n    A2 -->|WebSocket| WS1\n    WS1 -->|Validate Token| D\n    WS1 --> WS2\n    WS2 --> WS3\n    WS3 -->|Buffer Ops| HS2\n    WS3 --> WS4\n    WS4 -->|Broadcast| HS1\n    HS1 -->|Push to Clients| A2\n\n    WS2 -->|Snapshot| CS1\n\n    WS2 -->|Session Events| K\n    K -->|Publish| L\n    L --> P\n\n    C -->|Authorized| F\n    F --> I\n    I --> H\n    I --> J\n    J --> K\n    L --> M\n    L --> N\n```\n\n## Architectural Risks and Bottlenecks\n\n### Risk 1: WebSocket Server as Single Point of Failure (SPOF)\n\n**Description:** The proposed Whiteboard Service introduces a stateful WebSocket server. Unlike the stateless HTTP API which can be horizontally scaled trivially, WebSocket connections maintain persistent state (room membership, connection handles).\n\n**Impact:**\n- If the server crashes, all active whiteboard sessions disconnect\n- Horizontal scaling requires sticky sessions or distributed room management\n- Memory pressure from thousands of concurrent connections\n\n**Mitigation Strategies:**\n1. Deploy behind a WebSocket-aware load balancer (HAProxy, Envoy) with consistent hashing\n2. Use Redis Pub/Sub for cross-instance message propagation\n3. Implement reconnection logic with operation replay from Redis Streams\n4. Consider a CRDT-based state model (e.g., Yjs, Automerge) for conflict-free merge on reconnect\n\n### Risk 2: Eventual Consistency Between Whiteboard and Main Platform\n\n**Description:** The whiteboard operates on a separate data path with Redis as the source of truth for real-time operations. The main PostgreSQL database only receives periodic snapshots.\n\n**Impact:**\n- If snapshot fails, recent drawing operations could be lost\n- Search index and analytics may lag behind actual whiteboard state\n- Race conditions when correlating whiteboard events with other platform events\n\n**Mitigation Strategies:**\n1. Implement Write-Ahead Logging (WAL) to Redis Streams before broadcasting\n2. Use Redis persistence (AOF) for durability\n3. Emit fine-grained events to the main event bus for critical state changes\n4. Design idempotent consumers that handle out-of-order or duplicate events\n\n### Risk 3: Backpressure and Throughput Limits Under High Draw Rate\n\n**Description:** Real-time collaborative drawing can generate hundreds of operations per second per user. With 10 users in a session, this could mean 1000+ messages/second through Redis and WebSocket broadcast.\n\n**Impact:**\n- Redis Pub/Sub has no built-in backpressure; slow consumers drop messages\n- WebSocket send buffers can overflow, causing disconnections\n- Network bandwidth exhaustion on server instances\n\n**Mitigation Strategies:**\n1. **Operation Batching:** Aggregate drawing operations into 16ms (60fps) frames\n2. **Delta Compression:** Send incremental strokes, not full canvas state\n3. **Adaptive Quality:** Reduce precision or sampling rate under load\n4. **Rate Limiting:** Enforce per-user operation limits (e.g., 100 ops/sec max)\n5. **Sharding:** Partition large whiteboards into regions handled by separate processes\n\n## Summary\n\n- **Primary finding**: EduPulse Live follows a well-structured **Event-Driven Architecture** with **CQRS** patterns. The system uses RabbitMQ/NATS as the message broker, PostgreSQL for write models, and Elasticsearch/MeiliSearch for read-optimized search. The **Transactional Outbox** pattern ensures reliable at-least-once event delivery.\n\n- **Key components**:\n  - API Gateway: `module_49.txt` (Actix-Web), `module_1.txt` (Axum)\n  - Authentication: `module_1.txt` (JWT + Redis sessions)\n  - Event Publishing: `module_2.txt`, `module_4.txt`, `module_5.txt` (RabbitMQ/NATS publishers)\n  - Write Model: `module_22.txt`, `module_65.txt` (PostgreSQL repositories)\n  - Event Consumers: `module_15.txt` (Badge awarding), `module_76.txt` (Search indexing)\n  - Outbox Processor: `module_11.txt`, `module_50.txt`, `module_77.txt`\n\n- **Architectural pattern**: Event-Driven Architecture + CQRS + Transactional Outbox + Service-Repository Pattern\n\n- **Recommendations for Whiteboard Integration**:\n  1. Deploy a dedicated WebSocket microservice (tokio-tungstenite) for sub-50ms latency\n  2. Use Redis Streams for operation ordering and Redis Pub/Sub for cross-instance broadcast\n  3. Persist snapshots to PostgreSQL every 30 seconds for durability\n  4. Integrate with existing JWT authentication for session validation\n  5. Emit high-level session events to RabbitMQ for analytics and notifications\n  6. Implement CRDT or OT algorithms for conflict-free collaborative editing\n  7. Plan for horizontal scaling with sticky sessions and distributed room state\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":331,"cache_read_input_tokens":146578,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":331,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25,"service_tier":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"be6d8ca1-b4a5-4af5-85d5-dd0df50a0e18","uuid":"c721a88a-d1cc-4f58-9c63-c725a73a4116"}
